# Tips notebook

Everything learned from 59 games, with evidence. Built by `uv run tips.py build`; the players' sheet is distilled from the active entries.

## Active (303)

- [t5] (Everyone) Keep your votes consistent with your stated reads. If you change your mind, say why before the vote; an unexplained switch gets cited against you. Ballots are secret, so the record is your stated vote plus the announced totals. Village: a read/vote mismatch is a weak lead, not a verdict. Confused townies mismatch often, and a Fixer can flip a player's trial votes without telling them (nomination votes can't be fixed). Check a mismatch against claims, mechanical results and PM links. Never shoot or hang on a mismatch alone. Never hang a credible power role (e.g. a Veteran whose alerts killed visitors) over an old stated Yes on a nomination: a Yes only opens a trial. When a group pushes a mismatch case, count whether its Guilty votes alone could decide the trial, and ask who benefits. | support: 33 games, total weight 12.57 | against: 8 reports, total weight 1.97: Moderator: the read/vote mismatch scan led Dave to shoot Cardinal Grace, and her curse killed Alice and Frank.; Grace: Bob's misstated vote on his own nomination was a real slip, but the Yakuza turned it into the case that hanged a Vanilla. A mismatch is a lead, not a verdict.; Moderator: Dave and Grace hanged Vanilla Mallory on D6 at 5 alive mainly for two ballot/stance mismatches. That mis-hang handed the Yakuza 2 of 3. (The game had public ballots.); Ivan: the town treated Mallory's mismatch as near-proof and hanged her, despite the tip's warning.; Carol (Yakuza) filed 'hurt': the team's consistent, predictable reads made it look coordinated. The real problem was the D2 bloc vote, not consistency.; Bob filed 'hurt': his silent no-claim posture made nominations of Ivan and him predictable. Misapplication: the problem was refusing to claim, not vote consistency.; Grace and Heidi: the Village spent D3-D5 renominating on Carol's explained mismatch, and the Yakuza bloc turned the same mismatch argument against Vanilla Ivan and Grace.; Bob filed 'hurt': his self-nomination after saying 'no nomination' was used to dismiss his slot math. Misapplication: the tip predicts exactly that cost.; Judy filed 'hurt' because her stated-lean/vote mismatch got her nominated. Misapplication: she broke the tip, and the 'only a lead' clause got her acquitted. | recent evidence: Bob: stating his Grace lean, then voting Yes only after the Lookout log, kept his vote readable.; Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank (0.16 each) and the moderator: Grace's stated Guilty on Judy followed by an Innocent vote, and Heidi's flip, were caught and used as leads. Dave added that hanging on a flip alone nearly hanged townies.; Moderator, Mallory, Erin, Alice: Ivan said he wouldn't repeat his Yes on Jester self-nominations, then voted Yes on Dave's D5. The broken promise hanged him. Ivan's 'hurt' filing is a misapplication: he broke the tip.; Carol: Bob and Judy used read/vote mismatches to force clearer public positions before trials.; Moderator: on D4 Frank argued publicly that Carol was a real Profiler and pointed at Judy, then cast the deciding third Guilty (3-2). The real Profiler died and the Yakuza won.; Moderator, Alice, Grace, Ivan, Dave: the 6-2 total showed that Judy voted Guilty on Heidi after saying she leaned Innocent. The town treated it as a lead only, and Vanilla Judy was rightly acquitted 1-6 after admitting it.; Bob, Dave: stated reads tied to votes kept the record auditable.; Frank: keeping his own vote consistent and checking the totals (3 Yes, 5-1 Guilty) confirmed the counts were honest.; Dave and Frank: tracking stated votes against totals kept mismatches as leads. Frank's flip-flop Yes-then-Innocent drew a nomination until he explained it.; Ivan: the Yakuza kept stated and cast votes matched, so vote checks only confirmed them. Bob's stated No against 9 Yes of 9 was cited against him at once.
- [t9] (Everyone) A visit shows a destination, not an action or alignment. A track or watch cannot tell protection from framing or a Katana attack. A Lookout sees only visitors to their target, so watching the Katana holder shows nothing about the holder's attack. Killing an attacker at night does not cancel their attack that same night. A Lookout never sees their own visit, but that only matters if the Lookout could be the holder. If the reporter has independent support (a Tracker confirms their visits, earlier reports checked out, the named visitors confirm themselves) and X is the only other visitor to a Katana victim, treat it as near-proof against X. The same applies when several visitors are named and all but X have confirmed harmless explanations: X is then the lone unexplained visitor. This holds even more when X first denied the visit, claims a role that doesn't fit, or was the sole visitor to victims on several nights. Don't let 'a visit isn't an attack' or the self-visit caveat acquit such a visitor. 'Corroborated' is the condition that matters. A lone-visitor report (watch, track or map) from one unverified claimant with no second source is only that claimant's word, and a Jester or Yakuza can invent exactly this pattern, 'denied visit' included. Give it no weight if it was held back, names the claimant's own accuser, or is contradicted by claimants who confirm each other (t296). | support: 34 games, total weight 15.79 | against: 2 reports, total weight 1.43: Moderator: the Lookout-can't-see-himself caveat was used to acquit Erin, the real Katana holder, on D3.; Ivan (Grunt, won): the Yakuza relied on the caveat as their core defense, and Erin was acquitted while she killed Peggy, Dave and Frank.; Heidi, Ivan, Niaj filed 'hurt': the Jester fabricated exactly the 'lone visitor who denied it' pattern with a fake map, and nine voters took it as near-proof with no second source. | recent evidence: Dave, Erin and Heidi: Judy's watch on Mallory showed only Frank. It was read as a visit that corroborated his audit, not as Mallory acting.; Moderator, Alice, Frank, Mallory, Erin: Bob's N2 watch named Grace as Katana victim Alice's only visitor, and Grace was the holder. Parity, not bad reasoning, stopped the hang. Heidi and Ivan (Yakuza) said it hurt their side, which is the tip working.; Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Judy: Ivan's visit to Erin was read as a destination (it was a frame), not as a Katana plan.; Moderator, Alice, Bob, Frank, Grace, Ivan, Judy, Mallory: Bob's N2 watch named Grace as Katana victim Judy's only other visitor. Mallory's 2/2 count corroborated it, and Grace (holder) was hanged.; Alice and Carol (0.16 each): Alice's watches were treated as destinations, not actions. Frank's protection visit to Heidi was kept separate from Heidi's result.; Dave, Heidi, Ivan, Niaj: the corroborated lone visitor to Katana victim Heidi narrowed the holder to Erin or Judy and drove the Judy hang.; Frank, Niaj: Katana-victim group logic narrowed the holder to Judy, then Erin.; Erin's lone sighting of Bob at Katana victim Grace, corroborated by Carol's FT counts and Dave's map, hanged Bob 10-1 despite his 'only Erin's word' defense. Endorsed by nearly every player and the moderator.; Alice, Grace, Mallory (0.16 each): Bob's watches matched Grace's real marks, which separated the real Lookout from the Roleplayer.; Alice, Bob, Dave, Ivan, Judy, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia: Erin's watch plus the FT/Cartographer graph narrowed N1 to Erin or Heidi. Heidi's 'my visit was a check' didn't acquit her.
- [t24] (Everyone) Settle duplicate claims with independent corroboration (a Lookout or Tracker result confirming the same destination, an audit, an exact-role check), not repetition or speaking style. Build a positive case for each claimant early. Roles stay hidden on death, so no flip will settle it for you. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.95 | recent evidence: Judy, Niaj and Heidi all noted this. Mallory's and Dave's results were what resolved Grace vs Frank.; Alice and Grace: settle duplicate Medic claims with a watch or track on each claimant's claimed nights, not with vote history.; Olivia, Ivan, moderator: the Frank/Ivan Scrutineer duplicate was never settled with an independent check or a role-only detail, and the real holder was hanged D4.
- [t26] (Everyone) Scrutinize a player who stays silent for days and then suddenly produces a concrete accusation right as the board changes. The timing of the pivot is the signal. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.19 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.16: Frank: Ivan's late Oracle claim was true, and suspicion over its timing helped get him hanged. Vote patterns, not timing, found the Yakuza. | recent evidence: Bob went from four days of 'no nomination' to pushing Heidi right after Frank flipped.; Ivan and Mallory: the timing of Carol's late Archivist claim and Mallory's will drew useful scrutiny.; Heidi went quiet, then made an aggressive endgame nomination of Erin, and was hanged.
- [t29] (Everyone) Since private messages are publicly visible, briefly summarize their content in public. Unexplained PM clusters right after claims or votes became a main source of suspicion. | support: 3 games, total weight 0.79 | recent evidence: Erin, Grace and Dave all noted the PM graph being used as evidence.; Erin and Ivan: Erin's audit-request PM to Heidi became a public point of suspicion.; Grace: PMs sent to voters on a suspect's Day read as coordination unless explained at once.
- [t30] (Everyone) Before accusing anyone from public data, re-check the ledger: who sent each private message (not just who received it), each player's stated vote against the announced totals, the exact wording of role results, and each death's announced cause against the dead player's last will. Roles are never revealed on death, so a will is only the dead player's claim, not proof: a Fabricator can forge it and a Mortician can hide it. One reversed fact can discredit an otherwise true claimant. | support: 8 games, total weight 2.57 | recent evidence: Six players and the moderator flagged this. Erin, the real Lookout, reversed who messaged Carol, lost credibility and was hanged 3-1.; Moderator, Grace and Ivan: Dave's transcript check corrected a reversed timeline about when Grace named her visitors, which saved the real Lookout on D2.; Bob and Erin: re-verifying the Oracle reports and the ledger kept Grace's and Bob's cases honest, and Rupert corrected a false 'Erin never explained her vote' claim.; Judy: Mallory's claim that Judy never answered Carol was checked against the transcript and corrected, which stopped a D1 mislynch pile-on.; Alice, Frank, Grace, Peggy, Ivan, Dave: Ivan's misread claim that Alice voted Innocent on Frank was corrected from the ledger, and she survived.; Frank and Ivan: keep nomination and trial ballots in separate, accurate records. A misstated vote history nearly drove endgame accusations.; Dave and Judy: Dave's forged Sheriff will contradicted his Tracker reveal. The town treated the reveal as authoritative and the will as forged.; Mallory and the moderator: Mallory nominated Bob citing a Judy visit report that was never posted. The false detail collapsed the D4 trial on the real holder.
- [t38] (Everyone) Treat the nomination and trial votes as separate decisions, unless a Clerk may hold an unused rig token. In that case a passed nomination can mean execution whatever the trial vote: vote Yes only on someone you'd accept dying, and vote No at the nomination if you are a likely target. At low player counts, don't vote Yes just to 'hear a defense'. The nominator's Yes is automatic, so don't read it as a stance. Before voting Guilty, name one concrete contradiction or mechanical fit in their claims, votes or results, and say why an innocent explanation no longer fits. A missing result or announcement that a role-block fully explains (a blocked prosecution, an empty investigation, 'no named target') is never a contradiction; check the rules before calling one. A contradiction doesn't separate a Yakuza from a Jester who wants the hang. If the nominee self-nominated, voted Yes on their own nomination, asked for the rope, or the case rests only on behaviour (vote-steering, PMs), run the Jester check first (t91), and don't let a recited 'contradiction' stand in for a mechanical case. Each of these counts as a mechanical case: an unexplained Evil result against a Vanilla claim; a denial reversed into a role claim; a group result (survey, census, map, archive slot math) whose other members are confirmed, so elimination names the remaining player; a corroborated lone visitor to a Katana victim whose claim doesn't fit; a visit count that leaves room for only one visitor on a Katana night; slot math plus matching blocks plus a refusal to claim any role. The result behind the case must itself be corroborated: by the players it names, by a second source, or by the claimant's proven record. One unverified claimant's map, count or watch is a claim, not a mechanical fit, least of all when it was held back and names the claimant's own accuser (t296). A corroborated case plus the suspect's refusal to answer a plain yes/no about their own visit or role is enough. A bare denial offered only once cornered on trial does not answer it. 'A dodge isn't a contradiction', 'name a contradiction in my posts' and 'what exact fact has been disproved?' must not shield them. The standard is a better explanation, not proof. If you said you would vote Guilty unless they answered and they didn't, vote Guilty. Never pad a case with a fact you haven't checked: one false detail lets the defense discredit the whole push. An Evil result explained only by an uncheckable claim (Ex-Con) is not a clean acquittal: test it before trusting it. Echoed concerns and corrected slips are not new evidence. Three Innocent trials end the Day with no hang, so give your trials to the strongest mechanical cases and convict on them. This standard decides which case to convict, not whether to hang at all. If one more night kill could let the Yakuza block every nomination (e.g. 2 Yakuza at 4 alive), hang the best case available today, even a 50/50 group result plus a claim wobble (t286). Waiting for a perfect fit then loses the game. | support: 45 games, total weight 24.19 | against: 28 reports, total weight 12.76: Alice and Ivan filed 'hurt': map eliminations were treated as 'contradictions', and Dave was hanged 4-3 on push-based reasoning. Partly a misapplication: the tip asks for a mechanical case, and the case against Dave was behavioural.; Moderator (0.4) and Dave (0.16) filed 'hurt': players recited the 'concrete contradiction' standard but filled it with an impossible one (Ivan's 'no night named' after only N1) and stalled for rounds demanding visit-based contradictions instead of analyzing the archive. Partly a misapplication, since t47 covers mechanics. Alice, Bob and Carol (0.16 each) said the standard helped.; Grace: the strict standard helped acquit holder Erin 5-5 on D4, and Erin then killed again. The moderator attributes the tie to Mallory's misread holder pair and Heidi's hidden Innocent. Erin (Yakuza) also filed 'hurt'; that is weak.; Carol: Frank and Erin recited the contradiction demand to stall for days. The moderator says the mechanical case held anyway.; Moderator filed 'hurt': Grace and Judy used 'vote only on checkable contradictions' as a shield, calling Grace's proven false token claims 'already corrected'. Niaj and Olivia acquitted her twice, then hanged Bob on lobbying alone. Partly a misapplication; see t268.; Judy ('hurt') and Mallory (0.16 each): voters labelled wording quirks and PM content 'mechanical fits', and three Village players were hanged that way. A misapplication of the tip's own standard, which t41 now addresses explicitly.; Olivia (Yakuza) and Frank: the Yakuza used the standard to stall D6 nominations. Grace was hanged on a 'mechanical gap' that was really a miscount plus a swap. See t280 and t281.; Moderator (0.4), Carol, Dave, Frank, Grace (0.16 each) filed 'hurt': Dave and Grace used 'name a mechanical fit' to refuse a 50/50 census hang of Erin on D3, the last Day the Village could pass a nomination. The 2v2 lock followed, and the Yakuza won.; Erin (Yakuza) filed 'hurt' because the town used the tip to hang her team. Weak: the tip worked as intended.; Bob, Carol, Erin, Frank, Heidi, Judy filed 'hurt': nine voters treated Jester Bob's uncorroborated, held-back map ('Heidi lone visitor to the Katana victim') as the tip's mechanical fit and hanged the real Lookout. The word 'corroborated' was ignored. | recent evidence: Every hang rested on a mechanical fit (lone visitor, profile without Sheriff, census not Good). The Jester check on Frank used mechanics and correctly rejected his claim. All players and the moderator endorsed this.; Moderator, Alice, Dave, Erin, Grace, Heidi: every Guilty on Frank named the same broken visit claim against Heidi's one-visitor count, a correct 5-2 hang.; Alice, Bob, Carol, Erin, Frank, Heidi, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia (0.16 each) filed 'helped': demanding a mechanical fit separated the token-contradiction case on Grace from lobbying-only cases.; Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank, Heidi, Grace, Olivia (0.16 each): demanding a mechanical fit exposed Judy's watch claims against Erin's and Grace's own logs and drove the correct D3 hang.; Alice, Bob, Dave, Erin, Heidi, Ivan, Mallory, Niaj (0.16 each): demanding a concrete fit anchored Olivia's 11-1 hang on Dave's Evil check and exposed thin pushes.; Alice: demanding a concrete fit kept Frank's D3 trial from hanging a Good player (acquitted 3-2).; Moderator, Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Frank, Heidi: both hangs (Erin 5-1, Grace) were argued on stated mechanical fits and hit both Yakuza. Frank notes the Katana-kill argument was weaker than presented.; Alice, Carol, Grace, Judy, Niaj, Olivia, moderator: all three hangs rested on concrete mechanical fits and all hit Yakuza.; Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank, Heidi (0.16 each): asking for a concrete fit before Guilty separated Alice's Yakuza-only slip from weak pushes. Dave notes it did not stop the D4 mis-hang of Erin.; Dave, Grace, Mallory: read properly, the tip would have saved Heidi. Grace and Erin applied it and voted Innocent because the map was uncorroborated.
- [t47] (Everyone) Get the mechanics right before calling something a contradiction. Night actions are optional unless a role says 'must'. Two players can claim the same role unless the rules make it unique, or unless a role list plus confirmed roles (self-proving effects, exact-role checks) accounts for every player. Once every unshown slot is filled that way, a role listed once exists exactly once, and a second claimant is a provable liar. Roles stay hidden on death, so a dead player's slot stays open unless their role was confirmed. But 'roles can repeat' only means a claim isn't disproved; it is not evidence for it. A claim of the exact role a dead victim had claimed, made by a player seen visiting that victim the night they died, is still close to a confession: weigh it with the other evidence and don't acquit on the bare possibility. Count living players and elapsed nights as of the right moment: on Day 2 only Night 1 has passed, so 'name the night of your result' is not a contradiction, and demanding it repeatedly only creates false certainty. A false 'contradiction' costs the accuser credibility, and a real one dismissed as 'roles aren't unique' frees a Yakuza. | support: 29 games, total weight 10.26 | against: 2 reports, total weight 0.85: Moderator: the 'roles aren't unique' caution was misapplied to a full census and nearly acquitted the fake Sheriff Heidi.; Moderator (0.4), Ivan and Alice (0.16 each): the 'roles can repeat' caution was the stated reason two Good voters acquitted Pharmacist Erin's fake Sheriff claim on D2, which cost Heidi's life. | recent evidence: Judy: Ex-Con reads Evil and the holder reads Good, which explained her Carol/Erin split rather than proving a fake.; Dave, Erin, Frank, Grace, Judy, Mallory: getting mechanics right (Diviner one pick, Ex-Con random visit, Seer and Tracker not clashing) avoided false contradictions and exposed Frank's fake group.; Heidi and Judy: rule checks on alert and watch visits kept Dave's alert account and Judy's watch from being wrongly dismissed.; Alice, Bob, Grace and Judy: duplicate Vanilla claims and the Grunt slot were handled by checking mechanics, which avoided a false contradiction against Ivan and Grace.; Alice, Bob, Judy: checking the mechanics of the counts settled several false accusations.; Dave, Grace, Mallory: checking count mechanics (three visited can have four visitors) kept the FT debate on facts.; Alice, Dave, Frank, Carol: the claim that a Katana holder can't also divine was correctly rebutted, which kept the debate on real contradictions.; Carol and Frank (0.16 each): 'roles can repeat' answered Grace's stacked-claims accusation and Carol's slot math.; Judy and Ivan: Judy's timing check (Ivan was hanged before the N4 ignite) narrowed the Arsonist to Bob. Judy failed to apply the same check to the blocked prosecution.; Ivan, Carol: checking optional and Astral mechanics kept the table from hanging on false mismatches (Niaj's N2 pair, Dave's lists).
- [t69] (Everyone) Fortune Teller numbers are labeled: players visited (distinct) and players who visited anyone (distinct). Every visiting action counts, including Lookout watches, Tracker tracks, Accountant audits, Medic protections, Fabricator frames and the Katana. Self-visits count in both. A Veteran's alert isn't a visit, but anyone who visits the Veteran is. Blocked and Astral actions (Oracle, Ritualist, ignite, map, Coroner exam, Jester or Executioner revenge) count in neither. Players who died that night still count. One visitor can add several destinations: a Katana holder who also uses their own visiting action, a Smuggler (a swap visits both players) or a Seer's chosen pair (visits both). Read small counts literally. On a night with a Katana death, a visitor total of 1 means the holder made every visit that night. Anyone who claims a visit that night is then the holder or is lying, and a player who refuses to state their night action, or gives only a bare denial, is the hang. A totals-only role can't name visitors, so 'the FT's visitors are unnamed' is not a flaw, and evil uses that line to hang a real FT. FT: line your totals up publicly against every claimed visit, then ask each player with no claimed action for a plain yes/no on the leftover visit. A total that doesn't fit shows that some set of claims is false, not which claim. List every account that repairs it (holder acting twice, a swap, a chosen Seer pair, Ex-Con random visit, douses, switched plans). Outside the one-visitor case, never hang on the count alone, least of all against an exact-role check. Ask for the totals per night and the derivation before calling a count a lie. | support: 9 games, total weight 3.93 | recent evidence: Frank held the Katana on N2, attacked Grace and divined Mallory, giving 6&6. Judy read that as 'no double visits', ruled out Ivan's real token visit, and the town hanged Ivan, the real Philanthropist. Judy, Ivan, Frank, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Niaj and the moderator all reported this.; Alice and Carol: keeping the counts as unordered totals avoided false certainty and avoided a hang on counts alone.; Dave, Erin, Judy: listing every visitor (Katana holder, audits, tracks) was needed to reconcile Ivan's 2/2 and 2/3 with Alice's group.; Ivan and Alice: Dave wrongly claimed the Fortune Teller had a night action, and Ivan's 2,2 was dismissed rather than reconciled against visits.; Bob, Alice, Judy: reconciling Heidi's 3/3 and 4/4 required counting the Lookout's own visit correctly. Most of the 'open' visits came from counting errors.; Mallory: FT totals are a consistency check that rejects claims needing too many visits, not proof of a named visit.; Moderator, Mallory, Heidi: the N1 2/2 fit Bob's watch plus Grace's divine and left no room for Judy's claimed check. The N4 and N5 1/1 counts on Katana nights made the lone visitor the holder, and Heidi's N3-N4 denials and skipped N5 answer hanged her.; Moderator, Alice: Alice's N3 4/4 minus the claimed visits (Bob, Grace, Carol) left Frank or Heidi. Her N2 counts plus Ivan's trio left Dave, Heidi or Judy. Heidi, on both lists, was the holder. Alice adds: test every split of the unlabeled numbers before committing.; Carol's counts (e.g. 3/6) lined up against claimed visits corroborated Erin's lone visitor and left no room for hidden Yakuza visits.
- [t79] (Everyone) Before each night, write your planned target into your will (e.g. 'N2 plan: audit X'), so your death still yields info. The result of the night you die never reaches your will: results and deaths resolve together, and you can't edit at night. So post high-value results publicly while you're alive, and keep the will to past results plus tonight's plan. Label plans as plans and hedge accusations: a will can't be updated after death, and a confident line can hang a villager. Town: a will's 'plan' is not proof the action happened, since players switch targets. | support: 33 games, total weight 17.00 | against: 3 reports, total weight 1.32: Olivia: against a Mortician the N1 and N2 victims' wills were cleaned, so posting results publicly by day mattered more than the will.; Dave filed 'wrong', saying will-planned targets made his reads predictable. Weak: the moderator notes the opposite, since his N3 map on Judy was lost because his will didn't name it. The revisions he cites were public posts, not will plans.; Niaj filed 'wrong': his will held results but no plan, and he saw no benefit. Weak: he didn't follow the plan clause. | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Mallory and the moderator: Bob's, Alice's and Mallory's wills kept the Lookout, Scout and FT data public after they died.; Moderator: Bob's, Carol's and Alice's wills kept their Lookout, Oracle and Scout results public after the Katana killed them. Carol's will cleared Bob's claim and kept the table off Dave.; Moderator (0.4): Grace's will said 'later audit results will be posted by day', she died N3 auditing Ivan, and her 2-token result that would have saved him was lost. Bob and Grace (0.16 each) also endorse.; Moderator: Dave tracked holder Ivan, and Bob took his census around Erin (2 of 3 Evil), on the nights they died. Neither will named the target, so the info was lost.; Alice: wills held plans and past results, and results were posted by day.; Alice kept a full census log in her will and flagged Frank before N4, so the case would have survived her death. Judy's will named her Archivist plan before N1.; Dave: Grace's N3 Alice exam died with her, as the tip predicts. She held it for a planned post instead of the will and the morning.; Moderator: Bob spent his chosen Seer pair on Erin/Grace (both Yakuza, 'different') and was killed that night. His will said only 'no confirmed information', so the result was lost.; Erin and Mallory died with 'TBD' targets in their wills, losing Erin's 'Ivan visited Carol' edge and Mallory's N1 block log.; Grace: her N2 audit of Bob (0 tokens) died with her, as the tip warns, but her will's plan and expected counts helped the survivors read the night.
- [t85] (Everyone) On trial, lead with one concrete, checkable alternative explanation of the evidence, e.g. the exact visit assignment that fits the numbers. A counter-accusation reads as deflection; a worked example that collapses the case can move votes. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Ivan's reflection on his hanging over FT counts.
- [t88] (Everyone) Anyone fabricating a night account (Yakuza, Neutral killers) must test it against the global visit arithmetic, not just individual witnesses. Keep a ledger of claimed visitors, distinct destinations and token counts. Claiming protection on an already crowded target erases the extra destination your real kill created. Yakuza: choose the Katana holder so the night's visits match the team's public claims. A holder whose own action hits the kill target counts as one visitor. | support: 3 games, total weight 3.00 | recent evidence: Alice, Olivia and the moderator: the holders were chosen deliberately, and Judy's counts fit the Yakuza stories.; Bob and Frank: with an FT and a Cartographer posting, every unexplained Yakuza visit (Katana or own action) became a counted slot. A Diviner claiming no visits must skip, or pick a target already explained.; Bob and the moderator: the holder should be the member whose claimed visit lands on the victim. A no-visit holder added an exposed edge.
- [t120] (Everyone) Argue comparative likelihood, not mere mechanical possibility. A legal alternative explanation is not evidence that it happened, and 'some other claim could be false' rarely beats several converging clues. Don't count several consequences of one disputed claim as independent confirmations. On defense, name the specific weak premise, explain why your account fits better, and offer a concrete test that tells them apart. | support: 3 games, total weight 2.16 | recent evidence: Carol, Ivan, Frank, Rupert, Niaj, Dave and Grace all made this point. Bob, Judy and Heidi's 'mechanically possible' defenses all failed.; Rupert's 'that claim is uncheckable, test it first' failed against a self-testing Oracle hit; Heidi's 'mechanically possible' second-Pharmacist defense failed too.; Alice and Bob: repeating 'unverified' changed no votes. Name the exact conditional link and ask the accused to disprove it, or offer a concrete alternative explanation.
- [t132] (Everyone) Keep exact-role results separate from alignment. An Oracle WRONG rules out only the guessed role, not a faction, and a Roleplayer shows as 'Roleplayer' to exact-role checks. A RIGHT clears alignment only if the guessed role is a Village role. Use the miss to test the target's claim: it convicts when it disproves their claimed role and they then change their story. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.62 | recent evidence: Heidi, Ivan, Mallory, Rupert, Judy and Bob: Dave's wrong guess 'Bob = Pharmacist' was correctly not read as Evil, and Bob (Cardinal) was spared. His wrong guess 'Erin = Vanilla' disproved Erin's claim, and her pivots then convicted her.; Judy: a correct guess confirms the role only. Her Jester RIGHT on Carol voided Carol's fake Sheriff result.
- [t137] (Everyone) Know which claimed results are automatic (a Seer's nightly pair) and which need tokens or actions. Ask promptly about an unreported automatic result: it is a concrete, testable question. But treat the gap as something to be explained, not proof of a lie, and remember random pairs can repeat. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.17 | recent evidence: Erin, Ivan, Frank, Heidi, Dave: Heidi's missing N2 pair was pressed, and she was saved once she posted it. Frank and Heidi noted that repeated pairs are legal.
- [t141] (Everyone) Get the reason for a kill or a missing kill right before arguing from it. A role-block on the victim doesn't stop the attack on them; only blocking the Katana holder does. A kill can also fail to protection or innate Defense. List every explanation before building a case or a cover story on one. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.18 | recent evidence: Niaj wrongly argued that Olivia's block on Alice cancelled the Katana, which hurt his credibility. Olivia, Ivan and Heidi warned against overconfident causal stories.
- [t142] (Everyone) A Philanthropist's gift notice tells the recipient only that some Philanthropist acted, not who. A claimed recipient's 'confirmation' doesn't verify a particular Philanthropist claimant, and a token count or will written before the gift may be out of date. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.34 | recent evidence: Dave, Mallory and Olivia all stressed reading gift feedback literally.; Erin: track gifts as submitted actions, and record separately what the recipient's later account or will actually confirms.
- [t146] (Everyone) Before accepting a chain of claims that corroborate each other, list which links are independently verified. Verified links include a self-proving action, a token audit, a tracked visit, an Oracle check, a survived attack that matches a Medic's claimed protection, and a block notice confirmed by its target. A chain with no verified link is a story, not evidence. Name which single link could be tested tonight and what would still follow if it were false. Don't swing to ignoring the map, though. If its links hold up and it leaves one living candidate, that candidate stays the top case. Don't acquit them and then spend the hang on someone whose checkable record is clean but whose nominations misfired. Make the killer's hang the only goal and win the Jester's vote by negotiating, not with ultimatums. Pass his self-nominations early (a Yes only starts the trial), so the killer must vote publicly on any hang he promised, and expose a broken promise while you still have votes. If the numbers allow hanging the Jester without leaving you alone with the killer, that is also an option, because his win doesn't block yours. - [t192] (Everyone) Check every claimed neighbor-group result (Surveyor, Diviner) against the circle as it stood at the start of that night. A group with the wrong neighbors exposes a fake claim at once, and it is a clean mechanical contradiction. A Census Taker's group is different: its target plus two players drawn at random from everyone else alive, so no seating check applies to it. - [t218] (Everyone) Know which actions are Astral: Oracle and Ritualist guesses, the Juror's and Scrutineer's checks, the Arsonist's ignite, the Cartographer's map, the Coroner's exam, and the Jester's and Executioner's revenge attacks. No Lookout, Tracker, Fortune Teller, Insomniac, trap, Veteran or Katana holder can see or react to them. 'My target would be exposed' is never a reason to withhold an Astral result. A Smuggler's swap is different: it moves every action aimed at a swapped living player, Astral ones included. An Oracle guess, a Juror pair, a Scrutineer pick, a map or a Ritualist guess aimed at one swapped player lands on the other and is reported under the chosen name. Only random-draw effects (a Jester's or Executioner's revenge, a Cardinal's curse) are not moved. Yakuza: an Oracle isn't a visit-reader. - [t249] (Everyone) Vote totals can be changed on purpose. A Ballot Stuffer's trial vote counts twice, so the total exceeds the voters. A Fixer secretly flips one player's Guilty/Innocent votes for a Day: the total stays the same, the player isn't told, and their reminder shows what they cast. So a trial total that contradicts honest stated votes may be a fix, not a lie. When a trial total doesn't fit, first ask every player who stated a vote to re-check their private reminder and repost it. A misposted public vote is the commonest cause, and a phantom-Fixer hunt can eat a whole Day. Only then list the fix and stuffer accounts before accusing. An Auditor (Village) and the Yakuza's Informant see fixed votes as counted. Neither role touches nomination (Yes/No) votes, so a Yes count that contradicts stated votes is the cleanest evidence of a hidden liar. A liar is not necessarily Yakuza, though. A nominee who votes Yes on their own nomination and then claims No is baiting the hang, which is a Jester tell (t91), not grounds to hang. Auditor: audit a player whose votes decided a close trial or who defended a suspect. Post night, target and the full record, and read a trial-vote mismatch as 'lied or fixed' but a Yes/No mismatch as a lie. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 5 games, total weight 1.52 | against: 2 reports, total weight 1.32: Bob ('hurt') and Frank ('wrong', 0.16 each): the Fixer theory on a 5-vs-4 gap was a misposted vote and wasted a Day.; Moderator, Carol, Erin, Mallory filed 'hurt': the 9-of-9 Yes count against Bob's stated No became the lead reason to hang him. The test caught a liar, but the liar was a Jester who wanted to be caught. Bob and Heidi filed 'helped' as a liar test only. | recent evidence: From a revision, not a game. Cross the list with a Lookout's or Tracker's posted target and the claimed visits to narrow the holder to the living players who could be that role. - [t270] (Everyone) A nomination made within minutes of a claim, with a Guilty vote promised before the nominee has answered, is a tell worth noting. Ask the nominator once for the checkable reason, and hold your Yes until one is given. Don't hang on the pattern alone. Yakuza: never nominate fast with a pre-announced Guilty. It is the most readable pattern you can leave. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.80 | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Frank, Mallory, Olivia (Village) and Dave (Yakuza, 0.16 each): Dave's fast D3 nomination of Frank with a promised Guilty became the main pattern against the team.
- [t276] (Everyone) Before every endgame hang and every night kill, count who will be alive at the next Day start and compare it with the Vulture threshold, max(floor(N/4),2), where N is the starting player count. That is 3 for 12-15 players and 2 for 11 or fewer. A living Vulture wins alone at a Day start with that many or fewer alive, and everyone else loses. A hang or kill that drops the count to the threshold therefore hands the game to any Vulture still alive. The Vulture is Unique and usually hides behind a quiet, no-action Vanilla claim, voting with the majority. Village: while an unverified quiet claimant lives, don't let a mis-hang bring the count to the threshold; spend the hang on the best killer case or on that claimant. Killers: if the threshold is near, remove the unverified quiet claimant rather than a confirmed villager. Add this check to the Jester and Executioner checks before any late hang. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator, Bob, Dave, Frank, Ivan, Niaj, Alice: with 13 players the threshold was 3. The D6 hang of Vanilla Frank left 4 alive. The N6 Katana on Bob plus a missed Ritualist guess on Alice then left 3, and Alice (Vulture, quiet Vanilla claim) won. Bob noted that the Jester-focused check never prompted a Vulture count.
- [t281] (Everyone) Before calling anyone's vote-count claim false, agree which Day and which nomination or trial it refers to. Then count the voters alive at that vote: Yes plus No equals the living voters at the time, and dead players cast nothing. Include the nominee's own vote (a nominee may vote Guilty on themself) and any doubled ballot, then line each stated vote up against the total before naming a 'mismatch'. In a final three, recompute every past total this way before nominating, because one miscount can mislynch a Villager and hand the last killer the win that night. A tally from a different Day, or a miscount by the accuser, can hang a truthful player. Write the Day and the nominee next to every count you post or quote. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.96 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): Heidi read Grace's '9 Yes, 3 No' (Grace's own D3 nomination) as the D5 tally, and Juror Grace was hanged 8-2. Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Ivan, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia and Grace (0.16 each) all filed 'count living voters only'.; Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank (0.16 each): all filed 'count the nominee's own vote before accusing'. Dave's miscount of Erin's trial drove two nominations and nearly lost the game.
- [t289] (Everyone) Keep a day-by-day log of every role claim, not just votes. A claim that gains a night, a token change or a new detail each Day (e.g. a Veteran's alerts going N1, then N1-N2, then N1-N3) reads as improvised, so quote the earlier wording and ask for the full history once. But piecemeal posting is also what an honest, disorganized claimant does, so treat drift as a lead to test, not a hang on its own. Claimants: state your complete history (every night, target, result, tokens spent) once, in one message, and repeat it word for word. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Carol, Dave, Erin, Grace, Frank all flagged Heidi's growing Veteran alert log as a tell. Heidi was a real Veteran, so the drift came from piecemeal posting, not lying.
- [t291] (Everyone) Line up Fortune Teller counts, Cartographer sizes and every claimed visit. If the visitor count equals the number of claimed visitors (no spare slot), the Katana holder is hiding the attack behind one claimed visit. Usually it is a claimed visit that 'happened' to land on the victim (a check, a census, a watch), or a no-visit claimer linked into the victim's group. Say so outright: those claimants are the suspects. Allow for an Ex-Con's random visit as a floating edge. Split the suspects by claim order (a claim first posted after the numbers were public is the likeliest fit), by archive or profile lists, and by exact-role checks. Hang that Day rather than waiting a night. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Day 2: the 6/6 count, the 4,3,2,1,1,1,1 sizes and the posted targets left Erin (Lookout, claimed first) or Heidi (Sheriff 'check' on Katana victim Mallory, claimed last). Heidi was the holder. Reported by Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Ivan, Judy, Olivia.
- [t1] (Village) Never withhold a useful result to bait a counterclaim. Investigators: once you hold a result that bears on a vote, post the target and exact result, with as much of your role as the result needs to be believed and checked, in your first speech after getting it, or the moment someone asks, and keep your last will current. Naming your exact role feeds a possible Ritualist, whose guess kills through any Medic (t55): when one may be in the game, put the full log in your will before you claim and expect to die that night. A result held back and revealed after an opponent's claim looks retrofitted, above all when it names the player who just accused you. A hidden investigator who votes with the Yakuza looks exactly like a fake. Exception: with no result yet, or only a weak one, don't name your exact role on Day 2, and never say you will act or spend a token tonight. A firm exact claim of a role off the Roleplayer list (Bloodhound, Juror, Scrutineer...) is a sure Ritualist kill, so wait until the claim changes a vote. If someone else claims your role or exact result, counterclaim at once, in your next message, with your full log. Being second after a vague opener reads as copying, so ask the rival for the exact wording of their private result before you post yours. Never vote against your own information to 'wait and see'. If you must vote in a way that looks odd, explain why during that trial. | support: 24 games, total weight 11.66 | against: 6 reports, total weight 2.21: Dave: posting 'Carol Evil' on D2 marked him as an urgent threat. - [t11] (Village) Keep hostile Neutrals (SK, Arsonist) in the suspect pool all game, not only after the Yakuza die. Once an archive, survey or kill pattern suggests one exists, give every player a slot and keep a running list of candidates. Press anyone who admits a night action ('I took an action elsewhere') but won't name a role or target: treat it as a douse until explained. Press the quiet unclaimed players who give only measured, non-committal comments, and ask each for a claim. An Arsonist kills no one until it ignites, so quiet nights don't mean it's harmless; expect a multi-death night. A Not Evil Sheriff result or a Vanilla claim doesn't clear them. A failed attack with no block notice can mean innate Defense, not just a Medic. Doused Yakuza and Neutrals are told an Arsonist doused them, but Good players never are. - [t34] (Village) Once a player is shown to be Yakuza by a mechanical result (an Oracle RIGHT on a Yakuza role, a Yakuza-only role claim, a corroborated lone visit to a Katana victim), your top suspects are those who argued to delay or stop their hang, defended them, or exchanged PMs with them. A player merely caught lying is not shown Yakuza: the liar may be a Jester, and chaining from a proven liar to their early defenders lands on villagers. Roles stay hidden on death, so a hang never confirms who was Yakuza: keep such links only as strong as the case against the dead player. Ballots are secret, so before each nomination and trial vote, ask every player to state their vote publicly. Then compare the stated votes with the announced totals: extra No or Innocent votes mean someone hid a defensive vote. Do the vote math first. An Innocent bloc that couldn't change a settled result proves little, and Yakuza will reuse it to steer suspicion onto townies. Pushing No or delay at the nomination, then Guilty once the hang is certain, is the more telling pattern. A stated Guilty vote on a likely Yakuza clears no one. A bloc is a lead, not a verdict: a hidden investigator voting on their own info fits the same pattern, so demand a claim and results before hanging. If the game goes on after you have hanged everyone you believed Yakuza, either a Yakuza or a Neutral killer is still alive (Jesters and Executioners never block the end). Vote links to the dead then mostly point at Neutrals who voted alongside them, and a Jester wants exactly that hang. Switch to who could have made the remaining kills (causes of death, douse or attack visits), and press for claims. If you hang a likely Jester anyway, keep the Guilty votes to a bare majority. | support: 35 games, total weight 16.32 | against: 6 reports, total weight 2.93: Moderator: players applied it loosely to Carol's and Heidi's Innocent votes, which could not have saved Mallory at 7-3. - [t39] (Village) Investigators (Sheriff, Lookout, Tracker, Oracle) and protectors (Medic): never announce tonight's target in public or by private message. An announced target tells the Pharmacist or Party Animal whom to block and tells the Katana where to strike or what to avoid. Don't announce that you will act or spend a token tonight either, even with no target named ('I'll scent tonight', 'my audit will test him'): it marks you as tonight's kill. A Wiretap reads the contents of every PM, and nobody is told it exists, so treat every PM as read by the Yakuza. Never suggest another player's night target or test by PM. If someone PMs you one, don't reply confirming it; just act. Even a PM whose contents stay hidden marks you: everyone sees that it was sent, and Yakuza read a PM from one claimed investigator to another as a sign that both are worth killing. This covers only tonight's plans. Once a result exists and bears on a vote, post it in full (night, target, exact result). Never stretch secrecy about tonight into withholding or vaguening a past result, and never invent a mechanical excuse for it: Astral actions like an Oracle guess can't be seen by anyone. The one honest reason to hold a result is a possible Ritualist: an exact role claim is a sure guess through any Medic (t55), so a result that changes no vote yet can wait in your will. Narrow exception: a Trapper may announce a trap only to shield a confirmed investigator who is a likely kill target. Killers simply route around an announced trap, so it never catches anyone, and on any other player it is wasted. Otherwise keep the trap hidden and reveal it only after it fires. | support: 31 games, total weight 15.87 | against: 7 reports, total weight 2.44: Heidi: publicly naming Judy as a hang target from her census made her the obvious N3 Katana target and her N3 result died with her. The tip covers announced targets, not posted results; she still filed 'hurt'.; Moderator: Carol stretched the target-secrecy rule into withholding a past result, and claimed her Astral Oracle target could be 'exposed'. The evasion helped hang her.; Grace filed 'hurt': Judy and Heidi posted watch targets and counts, and Heidi was killed N4. Mostly a misapplication: the tip says to post past results, and the Katana kills investigators anyway.; Alice filed 'hurt': she announced her protection of Judy publicly. Misapplication, since she broke the tip; the attack on Judy still failed. Judy (0.16) said the tip helped.; Dave filed 'hurt' for announcing his alert plans. This is a misapplication: he broke the spirit of the tip, and t64 covers Veteran announcements. Frank and Grace (0.16 each) said the tip helped.; Moderator filed 'hurt': Bob turned 'never ask or suggest targets by PM' into a reason to hang Mallory and Frank, both Vanilla. This is a misapplication, since the tip governs your own conduct and isn't a test of alignment. t41 now says so.; Ivan filed 'hurt': secrecy about his Scrutineer target read as evasion. Partly a misapplication: the tip says to post results in full once they exist, and he posted only half. | recent evidence: Alice, Bob, Dave, Erin, Mallory, Niaj, Judy, Ivan (0.16 each): unannounced night targets left nothing to pre-empt, and Ivan's own public protection targets were the exception that cost him.; Alice, Heidi: Alice never named a nightly target, so the Katana couldn't plan around it.; Heidi publicly announced she would check Mallory, and Mallory blocked her every night after. Quiet investigators (Alice, Olivia) produced the winning census and profiles.; Dave: his claimed Lookout role and watch made him the next night's Katana kill.; Alice, Erin, Grace, Heidi, Ivan, Judy, Olivia (0.16 each, combined): no night targets leaked by PM all game.; Erin, Niaj, Olivia (0.16 each): kept night targets private. - [t41] (Village) Map who starts PM clusters and who co-nominates. If the same few players DM each other early and then push the same nomination with no new information, treat them as a likely Yakuza bloc. Once one of them is exposed by a mechanical result, use the shared stated votes and pushes against the others. But a PM link is a lead, never a hang on its own, and what a PM said is never a mechanical fit. Asking a claimed Lookout whom they'll watch, suggesting targets or testing a claim by PM is careless villager play that t39 warns against, not evidence of Evil. A villager who breaks that advice is still most likely a villager. A Jester or Executioner leaves PM trails on purpose: one player privately asking several others for Yes votes, or a lone early PM to a player later exposed as Yakuza, fits a Neutral as well as a Yakuza. A villager PMing for votes on a credible result is pushing clumsily. Judge the target on their own results, not on who pushes. Before hanging on a PM link or PM content, require stated votes that the totals back up, or a mechanical result that needs them to be a teammate. Look hard at quiet players who pile onto a PM-only hang. | support: 30 games, total weight 11.17 | against: 10 reports, total weight 3.72: Bob (Jester), Carol, Ivan and the moderator: Bob's lone D1 PM to Niaj (Pharmacist) was treated as proof he was Yakuza, and he was hanged 13-0. Jesters can exploit the PM heuristic deliberately.; Moderator: Heidi's vote-soliciting PMs were Executioner activity, not a Yakuza bloc. - [t43] (Village) Survival alone confirms nothing. A power-role claimant who survives a Katana attack may be real, since Evil rarely spends its Katana on a fake, but Arsonists and Serial Killers have innate Standard Defense that stops the Katana, and Jesters and Executioners have Basic Defense. Ask the survivor at once for one claim that explains their Defense (e.g. a Medic token self-protection, or a named protector). If no credible Medic claims the save, keep innate-Defense Neutrals in the pool and check the claim against token audits and visit logs. A survivor who first says 'innate Defense' or 'nobody protected me' and later claims Medic is far more likely a Neutral killer than a protected investigator. Role-blocks are different: only the blocked player is told, so 'I was blocked' is the claimant's own word and verifies nothing by itself. But it fully explains a missing result, so never treat that gap as a lie. Test the claim with an audit or a Lookout instead, and weigh repeated blocks reported before anyone pressed the claimant as mild support. | support: 21 games, total weight 8.11 | against: 9 reports, total weight 2.27: Ivan: charity toward plausible but unverifiable claims (Erin's Accountant) shielded the actual Katana holder. Note that Erin was never attacked, so this is a misapplication.; Ivan: Carol's Katana survival and Grace's protection story were unverifiable, and survival can come from innate Defense or an unclaimed protector. The moderator (helped) says the block half was simply ignored.; Dave, Heidi, Grace (0.16 each): survival from Arsonist innate Defense was read as support for a Medic claim. - [t48] (Village) When an announced trial total (Guilty plus Innocent) exceeds the number of living voters, a Ballot Stuffer doubled a vote. Ballots are secret, so compare each side's total with the players who said they voted that way. The side with more votes than stated holds the extra vote, or a hidden liar. The extra vote can sit on either side, including the losing side and the nominee's own Innocent vote. This narrows the stuffer to that side's voters; it doesn't name one. Separate the candidates using each one's stated position against the totals, plus any token or claim evidence (an audit sees ballot tokens), before hanging. - [t60] (Village) An archive or partial survey is a sample, not a census. It shows that roles exist, never how many. Never say a duplicate claim 'proves' a liar. Say instead that it shifts the odds, e.g. 'only half the archive's entries are real, so three Vanilla claims against one listed Vanilla are worth testing'. Overstating the math gets a correct suspicion discredited and the trial acquitted. Once a list is public, keep a role inventory every Day and post it. The list is one claimant's report until something corroborates it. Of the N roles shown, floor(0.5*N) are real and the rest are fakes that may or may not be in the game. So a listed role may be absent, and a role listed once may have an unsampled second holder. Use slot math to decide whom to press for an exact claim and whom to audit, watch or guess. Then hang on the contradiction that test produces (e.g. a token count against a Vanilla claim, an Evil check), not on slot math alone. Roles proven by public effects (a prosecution proves a Prosecutor, a clean a Mortician, an ignite an Arsonist) are certain. Track Unique roles (Smuggler, Vulture) separately: once one is proven, every other living claimant of it is a proven liar. A claim made right after a result that only explains that result is not placed, so keep it open. When the inventory narrows to a few unclaimed players, demand an exact claim from each; a refusal then is heavy evidence. Late in the game an unverified 'Vanilla' claim is worth testing early. Keep deductions conditional: corroborating one entry doesn't verify the whole list. | support: 14 games, total weight 7.26 | against: 4 reports, total weight 1.70: Judy: an archive is a random sample that includes fakes, not a census. - [t80] (Village) Accurate information proves access, not alignment. A Diviner can post real role results as a 'Surveyor', a Wiretap can quote real private messages as an 'Eavesdropper', and an evil player can truthfully expose a rival killer. A visiting killer can also present their own real visits (douses, a Katana hit) as 'Tracker' tracks, which then fit Fortune Teller counts and Cartographer groups perfectly. A fit with visit totals shows that the visits happened, not who made them or why. Before clearing an info claimer, ask which hostile role could produce the same evidence. Then look for a check that tells them apart: an Oracle guess, an Accountant audit, or a Lookout on the claimant's target. An exact-role result from an investigator with a proven record outweighs visit-count consistency. Confirming one detail authenticates only that detail. When such a claimer is exposed as hostile, re-examine everyone they cleared, but don't hang the subjects of their true reads just because they were 'confirmed' by them. | support: 4 games, total weight 3.72 | recent evidence: Frank, a Diviner posing as Surveyor, fooled the town for three days. - [t91] (Village) Jester warnings: suspect a Jester in a player who pushes hard with no evidence, self-nominates 'as a test', votes Yes on their own nomination (or misstates that vote), refuses offered tests of their own result, or makes a lone, easily disproved claim that contradicts a well-supported report and seems eager to be tried. A typical case is jumping onto a survey or archive pool's role so the real holder counterclaims. Track the conversion: if an archive lists Executioner and anyone has died at night, an Executioner whose target that was is now a Jester. An Evil Sheriff result on a Vanilla claimant with 0 tokens fits a Jester or Executioner as well as a Yakuza. A mild early dissent plus a lone PM to a player later exposed as Yakuza doesn't rule a Jester out. A caught liar is a liar, not necessarily a Yakuza, and reckless, checkable lies are what a Jester tells. If a more clearly Yakuza player is available, hang them first. While the Katana still kills, don't spend the Day on a proven liar who doesn't fit as a killer. Before any hang, ask whether the nominee wins by being hanged, and if so prefer to leave them alive. Naming the Jester risk aloud and then voting Guilty anyway is the worst of both. If you must hang, agree in public on a bare majority of Guilty votes and have everyone else vote Innocent. The revenge Divine-attacks a random Guilty voter, and no protection stops it. Confirmed power roles (Medic, investigators, a Prosecutor with tokens) must vote Innocent, and so must any Cardinal, whose curse would kill a second Good player. | support: 17 games, total weight 9.33 | against: 3 reports, total weight 0.48: Bob filed 'hurt': five days without hanging Judy let the Katana kill. - [t98] (Village) Judge a Roleplayer claim by its timing and consistency. A Roleplayer is told a fake investigative role and believes it: they claim that role from the start, never claim Vanilla, and never 'know' they are a Roleplayer. They can only suspect it, once their results clash with a confirmed claim or result (an exact-role check, a self-proving action, a verified log). A real one flags the clash early, before others claim the slot, and pushes against other Roleplayer claimants. A Vanilla claim that later becomes Roleplayer, an 'I know I'm the Roleplayer', or a claim made only once cornered or prosecuted is almost certainly a lie. When two players claim the same investigative role and their results conflict, consider an honest Roleplayer before calling either one a liar. If an archive, profile or survey shows 'Roleplayer', the duplicate claim may need no Evil player at all. Discount the claimant with the odder, unverifiable result instead of spending the Day hanging them, keep the other claimant's results, and spend the hang on the unclaimed pool. Also be suspicious when 'blind' results land exactly on the night's kill victims or fit claims made that same day. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.26 | recent evidence: Judy flagged possible Roleplayer on D3 before anyone else and was trusted all game. Niaj's Lookout 'results' landed on both Katana victims after Grace and Dave claimed. Erin went Vanilla to Roleplayer only once prosecuted. Both were hanged. Grace, Mallory, Dave and Carol reported this.; Alice, Frank, Olivia, Grace and Heidi all named Erin's Vanilla-to-Roleplayer pivot as mechanically impossible for a real Roleplayer. It decided the D7 hang.

- [t101] (Village) Delay-the-hang pitches ('tomorrow's test settles it, Innocent costs nothing', 'vote Innocent to expose a rig', 'no nomination before tonight's results') are classic Yakuza lines. Every night of delay gives the Katana a free kill, and when it kills an information role each night, the result you are waiting for usually dies with its holder. When a hang already has a corroborated mechanical basis, ignore rig-test pitches; the pusher is often the Clerk. Once a player is the best mechanical fit (named by slot math, fits the observed blocks or visits, refuses to claim a role), hang them that Day. Before granting a reprieve for a test, state which result would discriminate, what interference is likely, and what happens if the test doesn't occur. A blocked or missing check is no result, not a clearance. Ask whether the test resolves today's dispute; if not, hang the strongest case today. Exception: when the whole case is one unverified claimant's result against the accused, and the accused offers a specific test of that claimant's result, the test is worth the night (t296). - [t121] (Village) An Evil Sheriff result on someone who claims Vanilla, a role that doesn't explain the result, is a real case, but it fits a Jester or Executioner (both read Evil, both have 0 tokens) as well as a Yakuza. - [t227] (Village) A player who publicly claims a role that exists only on the Yakuza side (Grunt, Clerk, Diviner, Pharmacist, Puppeteer, Ritualist, etc.) has given you the strongest lead you will get. This holds even if they label it '(Village)' and withdraw it as a typo once called out, and above all when a public archive lists that role and nobody else claims it. Learn which faction every role belongs to, and check each claimed role against that list. Treat such a claim as a confession, not a wording slip. Accurate results don't clear it. Evil investigators like the Diviner get true information, and a player confirming one of their hits (e.g. a Vigilante confirming the drawn role) authenticates that result, not the claimant. 'It was a slip, show me a mechanical result' must not shield them. Nominate and hang them while the Village still has the numbers, and if you said you lean Guilty, vote Guilty. | support: 5 games, total weight 1.60 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Bob (0.16): Erin claimed 'Grunt (Village)' on D2, Bob's archive listed one Grunt. Carol and Mallory argued it was a slip, and the trial went 2-7. Bob's slot math put the Grunt among the unclaimed players. The town called it a lean, voted No on D3, and Erin held the Katana for every kill. - [t230] (Village) When a strong suspect is nominated, even by someone you distrust or by a possible teammate, vote Yes if you hold any real lead (a self-incriminating slip, slot math, an Evil result). Passing a nomination only opens a trial, and you still decide at the trial. - [t232] (Village) If you hold an exact-role result showing that a nominee is a Jester (an Oracle RIGHT, a survey or archive slot that pins them), post it in full (night, target, exact role, result) before the nomination vote, or at the latest at the start of the trial. Voting Innocent while hinting 'a hanged Jester wins' changes no votes. - [t236] (Village) Don't let an obvious Jester's self-nominations eat the Day. Each passed nomination uses one of the three trial slots, so vote No on a repeat self-nomination unless something new supports it. Agree early that a repeat nomination needs a new result. Sparing the Jester is not a reason to skip the day's hang: spend the Day's trials on the strongest Yakuza case (a count, a log, a refused night account). Exception: in a final three with the Jester holding the deciding vote, see t181. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.88 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): on D6, Erin and Frank voted Yes on Judy's self-nomination, and two of the three trials went to her while holder Grace survived. - [t238] (Village) Spend the Day's three trials purposefully. Nominate only someone you'd vote Guilty on if their answer fails, and state the test before nominating. A trial nobody intends to convict uses up a slot, reveals nothing and can end the Day without a hang. Keep a public count of the R14 stall clock (quiet Nights and Days since the last death), and when deaths stop, push for a real test or a mechanical nomination early in the Day. | support: 3 games, total weight 1.20 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): on D3 Bob, Carol and Frank were each tried and acquitted 0-8, ending the Day with no information. Carol and Frank (0.16 each) warned that stalling with unanimous Innocents pushed the game toward the stall rule.; Niaj: splitting trials across nominees produced 4-4 and 3-4 acquittals that spared the holder. Pick one checkable contradiction and push the whole Village onto it.; Heidi and Erin: chained nominations on single reads and bare answers (Dave, Alice, Erin, Frank) cost the town Villagers. - [t239] (Village) Under secret ballots, stated votes alone prove nothing: Evil players can announce Innocent and vote Guilty, or split their stated votes to look independent. At small counts, though, the totals can catch a liar. If 3 of 4 voted Guilty and the nominee surely voted Innocent, everyone else who said Innocent either lied or had their vote changed. Do this arithmetic after every small trial and publicly name whose stated vote the totals contradict. Before hanging, check for manipulation. A Fixer flips one player's trial votes without telling them, and a Ballot Stuffer adds a vote (compare the total with the voter count). Nomination Yes counts can't be altered by either, so they are the cleaner check, and an Auditor sees the vote as counted. If no manipulation fits better, make that player the next hang. | support: 3 games, total weight 0.96 | recent evidence: Moderator: on D5 Erin said she would vote Innocent on Bob and Heidi said Guilty. The trial still went 3 of 4 Guilty, which exposed Erin's lie, but the split made the two Yakuza look independent going into the final night.; Carol, moderator: the totals revealed Judy's hidden Guilty. Bob and Heidi caution that arithmetic resting on assumed votes is only a question to ask, not grounds for an accusation.; Moderator: the small-trial arithmetic found the hidden Guilty among the stated Innocents.
- [t240] (Village) A profile lists three roles in alphabetical order: the target's true role, one role held by another player alive at the start of that night (never the Profiler's own), and one role that no player in the game has. You aren't told which is which. Before posting a profile, ask the target to state their exact role publicly, so they can't tailor a claim to the list. A list that omits their claimed role is a hard, hangable contradiction, unless it shows Roleplayer and they claimed a role a Roleplayer can be told. Once the target's role is settled, the other two entries split: one is a living player's role and the other is absent from the game. So a Yakuza-only leftover (Clerk, Wiretap, Pharmacist, Smuggler, Ritualist, Diviner, Ballot Stuffer) is a strong lead that the role is alive, not proof, and two Yakuza-only leftovers mean one of them is. Match it to suspects and unclaimed players, e.g. a player who quoted a PM plus a Wiretap entry, and treat PMs as possibly read. If living players claim both leftover roles, one of those claims is almost surely false. Cross every profile with deaths, census results and confirmed claims to strike options. Post the full three-role list once it bears on a claim or a vote, and write it into your will as soon as you have it: a named Profiler is a sure guess for a possible Ritualist. Spend tokens on unclaimed players or on the contested side of a claim fight (e.g. two visitors to a Katana victim), whose role would decide a vote. Don't re-profile an active claimant to confirm them, since one profile never confirms a claim. | support: 3 games, total weight 2.56 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): Carol's N3 profile of Vanilla Frank listed Clerk and Wiretap as decoys, nearly proving Erin (who had quoted a PM) was the Wiretap. Nobody used it, Carol was hanged, and the Yakuza won. Carol (0.16): combine profiles with deaths to eliminate options.; Olivia's profiles on cleared players listed Diviner, Wiretap and Ballot Stuffer as decoys, proving those roles were in play. The town stopped PMing. Dave, Judy and Bob noted the same.; Olivia's profile of Erin listed Diviner as a decoy, pointing at Frank's Surveyor claim. Reported by Olivia, Dave, Niaj, Carol, moderator.
- [t241] (Village) When a player quotes or closely paraphrases a private message they weren't party to, only an Eavesdropper (who visited the sender or receiver the night before) or the Yakuza Wiretap could have read it. Ask at once which role, and which player they eavesdropped. The named target must be in that exchange, and the visit must fit any Lookout, Tracker or visit-count data. A claim that can't name a fitting target, or a role list or profile decoy showing Wiretap, points to the Wiretap. Don't vote on the quoted PM's content until the quoter's access is explained. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.88 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Alice, Carol, Heidi, Frank (0.16 each): Erin quoted Heidi's PM to Carol almost verbatim, and four players flagged it. Her untested Eavesdropper claim survived, the town never checked it against Carol's Wiretap decoy, and Erin won.
- [t243] (Village) When roles stay hidden on death, use the night kill list as a claim test. A claimed investigator killed by the Katana was probably real, because the Yakuza kill the threats they can't explain away. In a duplicate or conflicting claim (e.g. a Sheriff calling a Seer Evil), the rival who survives deserves the test (audit, exact-role check, watch), and a fake read calling the Katana victim Evil collapses. A Katana death shows the victim wasn't Yakuza, not that they were Good: a Neutral can die to the Katana too, and it doesn't confirm every claim they made. Weigh it against the possibility that the Yakuza killed a real townie to leave a fake standing, and act on it before the next night rather than after. A Roleplayer is only ever told Sheriff, Tracker, Lookout, Seer, Fortune Teller, Oracle or Accountant, so a Katana-killed claimant of any other info role (Census Taker, Coroner, Bloodhound...) can't be an honest Roleplayer. Don't spend a Coroner token or a Day verifying them: act on their posted result at once. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.32 | recent evidence: Alice and Grace (0.16 each): real Lookout Bob was the Katana kill after the D2 duplicate-claim fight. Archivist Carol and Vigilante Heidi died later the same way.
- [t245] (Village) Hunt Neutral killers on purpose; an unhunted Arsonist or Serial Killer often wins. Only killers that block wins matter here: a Bounty Hunter, Magpie, Jester or Executioner never blocks a Village win, and a Bounty Hunter who has spent its one token can't attack again. While the Katana still kills every night, never spend a Day's hang on a confessed or spent never-blocks-wins Neutral, and look hard at whoever rushes one to trial. Roles stay hidden on death, so the death announcements are your main evidence. Read every cause of death. An attack cause that isn't the Katana, a hanging, a Vigilante shot, a Veteran alert, a trap, a Bounty Hunter attack, a Jester or Executioner revenge or a Cardinal curse (e.g. a Serial Killer attack or an Arsonist ignite) proves that killer is alive. Two fatal causes on one victim mean two separate killers. A multi-death night, or more deaths than the Katana and known shooters explain, points at an ignite. Quiet nights don't clear an Arsonist, who kills no one until it ignites. From Day 2 keep a written Neutral-killer suspect list: unclaimed or Vanilla claimants nobody has watched, players who survived an attack with no credible protector, players whose visits leave unexplained gaps in counts or groups, and anyone who admits an action but won't name it. Point Lookouts, Trackers, Oracle guesses (Arsonist, Serial Killer) and audits (0 tokens fits both) at them. A Not Evil Sheriff read doesn't clear them. The game can't end while one lives, and at two alive you can't hang them, so spend a hang on the best SK or Arsonist suspect well before the endgame, but never ahead of a stronger Yakuza case. | PINNED: Kevin asked for it on the sheet | support: 4 games, total weight 1.56 | against: 3 reports, total weight 1.36: Moderator (0.4), Heidi and Ivan (0.16 each) filed 'hurt': the 'hunt Neutrals' line fed the D4 hang of spent Bounty Hunter Erin, who never blocks wins, while Yakuza Grace kept killing.; Frank, Heidi ('hurt') and Carol ('wrong'), 0.16 each: the later D5-D6 hunt drifted into PM-based hangs of Vanillas. A misapplication: the tip asks for mechanical pointers, not PM reads.; Frank filed 'wrong': no Neutral killer was in the game, only a Bounty Hunter. Untested, not evidence against the tip. | recent evidence: Added in a revision at the instructions' request, not from a game. It draws on t11, t159, t180 and t221 and is worded for hidden roles.; Grace: the town read the causes of death and kept the Katana/Sheriff picture straight. Weak, since no Neutral was in the game.; Moderator: the town read the Serial Killer causes of death (Dave, Heidi, the attack on Bob) and hanged Olivia, the SK, on D4. No SK kills followed.; Olivia, moderator: the D3 hang went to Jester Bob, who never blocks a win, while all three Yakuza lived. It was the Village's last real hang; D4 opened at 3 Yakuza of 4.
- [t251] (Village) A Seer pair becomes a mechanical case only through an anchor. An unanchored DIFFERENT pair shows only that the two read differently, which may come from an Ex-Con, a framed player or a Good-reading holder rather than a Yakuza. It is a lead, never a hang on its own. Once one member is independently confirmed Good (a Sheriff Not Evil plus an Oracle RIGHT, a self-proving action, a verified log), a DIFFERENT pair names the other as non-Good, so nominate them. After a hang, a Coroner exam confirming the hanged player's role extends the chain. A SAME pair between a confirmed Yakuza and another player points at that player too, unless the confirmed Yakuza held the Katana that night (then the partner read Good). Before the vote, say which anchor the chain rests on. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.36 | recent evidence: Moderator: Carol was anchored by Bob's Not Evil and two Oracle hits, so Niaj's N3 'Carol/Alice different' hanged Alice. Ivan's Puppeteer exam plus the N2 'Alice/Frank same' hanged Frank. Moderator: the decisive N4 random pair (Heidi/Carol differ) was anchored by Sheriff Heidi's N2 Katana survival and exposed Carol.
- [t255] (Village) Before nominating, count the stated Yes votes and nominate only when a majority is clearly there. - [t256] (Village) Test a late Jester or Executioner claim on trial with mechanics, not the revenge threat. (1) Win check: Jesters and Executioners never block a win. If the game didn't end when the last known Yakuza died, some non-Village, non-Jester player is still alive. If the claimant is the only plausible one left, the Neutral claim fails. (2) Night pattern: a no-kill night on which only the claimant was blocked fits them holding the Katana. (3) Foreknowledge: did they name a role before its owner claimed it? That points to a Yakuza info role (Diviner, Wiretap), not an Executioner. (4) True-alignment counts: a census 'not Good' fits Neutral or Yakuza, so it decides nothing alone but combines with the above. If these converge, hang with a bare Guilty majority and keep confirmed info roles on Innocent. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Frank (Diviner) claimed Jester on D5. Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Grace, Ivan, Judy, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia and the moderator all cited the same proof: the game hadn't ended at Heidi's hang, N4 had no kill while only Frank was blocked, he had claimed Oracle before Grace spoke, and the census read him not Good. Hanging him won the game.
- [t258] (Village) Note on your notepad anyone who claims or names a role before its real holder has spoken, e.g. a Day 2 Oracle claim made before the real Oracle claims, which then forces a counterclaim. Picking exactly the role a silent player holds suggests exact-role or PM knowledge (Diviner, Wiretap), not luck. It is a lead on Day 2. - [t262] (Village) Each morning, combine the visit-based results into one ledger: Fortune Teller counts, Cartographer sizes and named map groups, and Lookout or Tracker logs. Have every claimed visitor post their exact target, then reconcile. Together the counts fix how many hidden visitors exist and which groups they sit in. A single unexplained visitor, a 'no action' claim from a player in a group with an unexplained slot, or a missing kill on a block night almost always names the Katana holder. | support: 2 games, total weight 2.00 | recent evidence: Alice, Carol, Dave, Erin, Ivan, Judy, Mallory, Niaj: Carol's counts plus Dave's sizes and map plus Erin's watches left one unexplained visitor each night and drove all three hangs.; Niaj, Heidi, moderator: the combined FT, Cartographer and claim ledger narrowed the holder to 2-4 names each night.
- [t268] (Village) A claim already shown false stays false after the claimant 'corrects' it. 'Already corrected' is not an answer, and you don't need a new contradiction. Token claims are the cleanest test. A Magpie's victim is told which token kind was stolen, so a token count posted after a theft notice is a checkable false claim. An Accountant audit gives the true count. A Medic's Standard protection of another player is free, so '0 tokens' never explains a Medic protecting nobody while players die. Ask a token claimant once, plainly, whether they got a theft notice, and line their posted counts up against every audit and theft. If the story has changed to fit each new fact, hang them. Apply one standard to every nominee: don't acquit a broken claim and then hang someone else on lobbying alone. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.80 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4) plus Bob, Carol, Frank, Judy, Niaj, Olivia, Mallory and Alice (0.16 each). Grace (Yakuza) posted 2 Medic tokens after a Magpie theft notice, Mallory audited her at 0, and she never used free protection. She survived 4-4 and 3-4 as 'already corrected' and went on to kill four more Villagers.
- [t269] (Village) Work from the roles that actually exist. If nobody claims a visit-tracking role (Tracker, Lookout, Fortune Teller) after the first request, stop asking for visit logs and work from votes, token audits and claims. Vote-reading roles (Juror, Scrutineer, Auditor) need trial data, so a Day 2 trial gives them something to read. But 'a trial for vote records' is a reason to open a trial, never to execute. Agree beforehand that it ends Innocent unless a corroborated case appears, and don't put one side of a live claim duel in the dock for it. Expect little from the records when the Yakuza vote with the majority and state their real votes. The ballots mostly pay off once you have a suspected pair to compare. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.96 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Moderator, Mallory: Carol, Ivan and Frank asked for a Day 2 'trial for vote records', and it became Lookout Heidi's execution.
