Players kept asking for nonexistent Tracker and Lookout logs through Day 7. Frank (0.16): with no trial, a Scrutineer has nothing to scrutinize.; Moderator, Grace: D2 had about 70 'wait for Night 2' messages and no votes, so the vote-reading Juror and Scrutineer had nothing on N2.
- [t272] (Village) Read the Katana's victim list as 'who threatened the Yakuza', not 'who threatened today's suspect'. Keep a running list of each victim, whom they had last checked or challenged, what their next result could have shown about every living player (an audit reads any token-holder, a Bloodhound's trio count covers any attacker drawn into it), and who survives. When the Katana keeps killing whoever checked or pressed one quiet, uncontested claim, the player behind that claim and those who never challenged it are your leads. But when the victims were testers of a loud, already-disputed claimant who is left alive, don't conclude the kills protect him. - [t280] (Village) A Smuggler swap redirects every action aimed at either swapped player, whether or not it visits: the Katana, a Medic's save, a Sheriff check, a Seer pair, and Astral checks such as an Oracle guess, a Juror pair, a Scrutineer pick or a Cartographer's map. The result still names the player the user chose. Random-draw effects (a Jester's or Executioner's revenge, a Cardinal's curse) are not moved. Before hanging an investigator whose result looks impossible, or calling a survival or death story a lie, ask whether anyone posted a swap notice that night. Then recompute the result with the two players traded. A swap explains an odd result the way a role-block explains a missing one. Both swapped players are told, and Smuggler is a Yakuza role, so a swap notice proves a living Yakuza Smuggler. Post it the next Day. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4): Grace's N4 Juror check on Alice/Dave landed on Alice/Niaj because Niaj and Dave were swapped, and Niaj had posted his notice. The town still called her result a lie and hanged a truthful Juror. Niaj, Erin, Judy, Heidi, Frank, Ivan and Dave (0.16 each) all stressed tracing where swapped actions land.
- [t282] (Village) When the Yakuza could be near half the living players, assume a Fixer or Ballot Stuffer can turn any trial. Don't vote Yes on a nomination without a real mechanical case. Once the trial opens, one fixed Innocent vote becomes a Guilty and can decide it, even the nominee's own vote. Count the likely evil bloc first, and pass only nominations the Village could convict on its own votes. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator: at 3v3 on D6 Bob and Niaj voted Yes on Mallory's nomination of Heidi. - [t286] (Village) Count down to the vote lock. A nomination needs a majority of living voters, so once the Yakuza hold half the living players they can block every nomination by voting No. With 2 Yakuza that happens at 4 alive (3 Yes needed); with 3 Yakuza, at 6. The Katana takes one Villager a night, a Ritualist can add up to two more over the game, and a Jester's revenge or a Cardinal's curse adds others. Katana and Ritualist victims are never Yakuza, so unless a hang was confirmed Evil, assume every Yakuza is still alive. With 3 Yakuza in 13, two mis-hangs can bring the lock by Day 4, so the Day 2 hang needs a corroborated fit, not a claim duel, and the Day 3 hang must go to a kill-faction candidate. The Day before the lock is your last real hang. Put your best suspect on trial that Day, even on a 50/50 group result such as a census that leaves two names. Don't wait for a result that only arrives after the next night, because its holder is the likeliest kill. Post the count each morning: 'X alive, at most Y Yakuza, lock after Z more deaths.' | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | recent evidence: Moderator plus Alice, Grace, Dave, Carol (7 players, 2 Yakuza). On D3 at 5 alive, Dave and Grace voted No on Frank's nomination of census-suspect Erin to wait for Grace's D4 Coroner read. Grace died N3, every D4 nomination failed 2-2, and N4's Katana plus Ritualist ended it. A 3-2 hang of Erin was available on D3.; Carol, Dave, Erin, Frank, Grace, Heidi, Ivan, Niaj, Mallory: nobody counted down to the lock. Two mis-hangs plus double kills left the Yakuza with 3 of 4 votes on D4, and Ivan's nomination failed 1 Yes of 4.
- [t287] (Village) Ask every claimed Medic for each night's protection target the next morning, and line it up against the kill list. A real Medic usually covers the claimed info roles the Katana is likeliest to hit. A Medic whose protections keep landing on unattacked players while claimed investigators die unprotected is a strong Yakuza lead. A matching token count proves nothing, since a Ritualist also starts with 2 tokens. When a group result narrows a Yakuza to two names, look next at who shields the likelier one (urging 'don't pile on', voting with them): that player is the second-Yakuza lead. Turn it into a nomination, not just a remark. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.40 | recent evidence: Moderator, with Alice, Carol, Dave, Frank and Grace (0.16 each), all named it the strongest tell. Bob (Ritualist) claimed Medic, 'protected' never-attacked Dave on N2 and nobody on N3 while Census Taker Alice and Coroner Grace died. He said 'let's not pile on Erin' and voted with her. It was noticed but never voted on.
- [t292] (Village) Treat a role-claim switch made right after a map, count or census puts the player in a suspect group (e.g. 'I was Vanilla, actually I'm Medic') as a near-confession. It is exactly the cornered switch Yakuza are told to avoid. Spend that night's Oracle guess or profile on the new claim. A WRONG plus the switch is enough to hang. 'I hid as Vanilla on Day 2 to stay safe, as the tips say' doesn't excuse it. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Bob switched from Vanilla to Medic after Dave's N2 map placed him. Grace guessed 'Bob = Medic' on N3 and got WRONG, and Bob was hanged 7-1. Reported by Carol, Niaj, Ivan, Grace, Alice, Judy.
- [t293] (Village) Use a public archive list as a tiebreak once the evidence has narrowed to two rival claims. A real holder's role appears only if that player was sampled, and a fake appears only by chance among all roles. So a claimed role on the list is several times likelier to be real than one missing from it (roughly 5-10x in a 13-player game). That is enough to break a 50/50 the visit math already produced when a night's delay costs a kill. It is not a standalone case. A listed role is also a ready-made fake claim for the Yakuza, so a new claim of a listed role still needs a test. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Lookout (Erin) was listed and Sheriff (Heidi) wasn't. Judy and Ivan used the list to break the Erin/Heidi tie, and Heidi was the holder.
- [t295] (Village) An uncontested Sheriff's posted results are the Village's best information, so don't discount them because no second source exists yet. - [t296] (Village) A result from one unverified claimant that was held back and then names the very player who just accused them is retrofitted until tested, so don't hang on it. 'He posted his count before any visitor spoke' is not evidence either: a Jester or Yakuza can post a fake first and call every contradicting visitor a Roleplayer. When two independent claims confirm each other (e.g. an audit of 0 tokens matching a Lookout claim) against one lone counter, the story that needs two Roleplayers is the weaker one. If the accused offers a cheap overnight test of the counter's claim (a scent, an audit, a secret gift or visit the counter must report), vote Innocent and run it. A claimant who waves off a test of their own result is the likelier liar, possibly a Jester: test or ignore them rather than hang their target. | support: 1 games, total weight 1.00 | recent evidence: Moderator plus Carol, Dave, Erin, Grace, Heidi, Ivan, Mallory, Niaj, Olivia, Alice, Bob, Judy: Jester Bob's fake one-visit count and held-back 'map' named Lookout Heidi right after she named him. Heidi and Accountant Grace contradicted him and offered Dave's scent and Mallory's gift as tests. The Village hanged Heidi 9-3 on 'he claimed first', and that mis-hang decided the game.
- [t297] (Village) An Oracle claim whose log holds only 'wrong' guesses is uncheckable and is the natural Ritualist cover: both guess roles astrally, with no visits. Treat it as unverified, more so once a Ritualist kill has appeared, when its guesses land on night victims, when its owner dodges a Jester revenge pool, or when its first decisive 'WRONG' appears just as it is needed against a rival of another claimant. While the duel runs, point audits, scents and questions at those coasters. If a second player claims your role, treat them as a top suspect that Day, and don't assume the later claimant is the copier. Settle the duplicate the day it appears, while Good votes last. Ask each claimant for something only the real role sees, such as the exact wording and name order of the private result line, and get the rival's answer before the real holder posts theirs. It hands the accuser a ready way to dismiss you. State your result flatly and show that the rival's specific claim is false under every reading. - [t4] (Yakuza) Assume the real holder of any role you fake-claim is alive and will counterclaim, that an Accountant can audit your exact token count, that an exact-role check (Oracle, Ritualist, Diviner, Archivist, Surveyor or Profiler list) can rule your claimed role out, and that a public role list plus self-proving effects (prosecutions, cleans, ignites) and confirmed claims can leave no slot for it. Never claim a Unique role whose holder may be alive. Never copy a role whose actions were already public (e.g. a Prosecutor): the real holder will counterclaim with earlier reads and correct night labels. Never claim the role that a dead victim you visited had claimed in public or in their will. Pick a fake role whose tokens match yours: claim a tokenless role only if you hold no tokens. Never volunteer your token history or report more results than the role's tokens allow. Visit results include Lookout and Tracker logs, Insomniac role lists, Scout and Cartographer groups and FT counts. Before any claim that includes a visit, check it against every visit result already posted: a claimed track or watch that an existing count rules out (e.g. a 'track' on a night an Insomniac saw exactly one visitor, a confirmed Lookout) is exposed within a round. A no-action claim is unsafe if you visited a target someone may have watched, so prepare a story that fits a token check or visit report. If you fake a visiting role (Tracker, Lookout), name your real visit destinations as your targets so these results can only confirm you, and fix the claim in night chat before you need it. Never improvise targets. - [t16] (Yakuza) Don't defend a doomed teammate. Ballots are secret, but the totals are announced, and the town compares them with what everyone said. Plan the whole team's votes and stated positions across trials in advance. A doomed member's last words shouldn't defend or attack anyone in a way that maps back to teammates. - [t33] (Yakuza) Never speak publicly from your hidden perspective. Say 'died to a Katana', never 'my Katana'. Never state as fact anything you know only from night chat or the Yakuza action report: who held the Katana, who acted or was role-blocked, a teammate's result. This applies even when it's offered as a 'mechanics check'. It also covers endgame faction-count arguments: 'if you hang me, only Village is left' shows you know the team count. - [t56] (Yakuza) Chain exact-role info into Ritualist guesses. Firm public role claims and Wiretap-read claims are the sure ones, and a right guess never outs the Ritualist. A Diviner draw is the picked player's role only 60% of the time, so confirm it against claims or a second draw before guessing; a Mortician's clean names a dead player's role, which helps only by elimination. - [t57] (Yakuza) Give the Katana to a member whose own role matters least (Wiretap, Grunt), not your Diviner or other info source, but only if that member is also unlikely to be blocked or watched tonight. Never pass it to a member a blocker (above all a block-immune Party Animal) has already blocked or publicly said they will block. Blockers repeat, and you lose both the kill and that member's own token action. A holder with no visiting role (Vanilla claim) has no cover if a watcher places them at the victim, so agree a cover story before Day starts. Expect the obvious target, e.g. a player whose test you just learned about, to be watched. Move the Katana that same night to an unexposed, unblocked member when either of these happens: a Lookout or Tracker publicly names the holder as a visitor to a victim, or a Cartographer, Scout or Fortune Teller result narrows a victim's visitors to a few names that include the holder. The exposed holder will be blocked, shot or hanged, and the Not Evil read stops none of that. A holder who stays the lone visitor to victim after victim becomes the one name left on every elimination list. A confident organizer on the first Day (Day 2) is often a Medic or investigator and a good kill that night, but may be an alerting Veteran. - [t61] (Yakuza) Agree the team's claim set in night chat before Day 2. Once a role list is public, count the unseen slots first: subtract roles proven by public effects or exact-role checks and your own unlisted Yakuza roles (an unseen Mortician fills one). Assign at most that many off-list claims across the whole team, and never put two members in one slot or both on Vanilla. Never fake-claim a role the list shows once while its holder may be alive, and never a Unique role (Vulture, Smuggler) whose holder may be alive: their counterclaim disproves you for free. Roles stay hidden on death, so a listed role whose known claimant is dead is the safest choice. - [t176] (Yakuza) Never claim a role that exists only on the Yakuza side (Mortician, Clerk, Diviner, Puppeteer, Ballot Stuffer, Wiretap, Pharmacist, Ritualist, etc.), whether as a fake claim or as the truth. That includes a role-limited hedge like 'my Puppeteer role took no action' and any public use of Yakuza-only vocabulary ('Ritualist', 'night chat'), which names your role as clearly as a claim. Any Good player can point out that a Village player can't hold it, and one Sheriff check finishes you. Before Day 2, agree in night chat on a safe Village claim for each member. It must fit the unseen archive slots, your token count and your real visits, and it must hold up when a neighbour-based survey, a map group or slot math corners you. 'Village, no claim, no action' is not a safe claim in an info-heavy game: once the claimed roles are cross-checked, the one unclaimed member of a victim's group is named the holder. - [t248] (Yakuza) (Team with a Smuggler) Don't veto the swap out of fear it will redirect your own attack. Aim the Katana at a decoy and swap the decoy with the player you really want dead, whom the Medic is likely protecting. The Katana lands on the real target, and the protection aimed at them moves to the decoy. Before submitting, work through both cases (the Medic guards the real target, the Medic guards the decoy). The kill only lands if the save isn't on the player the Katana ends up hitting. Use 2: dodge an investigation. If you expect to be checked tonight, swap yourself with a Villager. Your own team's actions move too: a Ritualist guess or Diviner pick aimed at a swapped player lands on the other, and a guess that misses that way outs the Ritualist and spends the tokens, so tell the team who is swapped. Random-draw effects (a Jester's or Executioner's revenge, a Cardinal's curse) are not moved. Never swap anyone with the Katana's target unless you mean to move the kill, and never swap yourself with the target. The Smuggler visits both swapped players, and both are told they were swapped. - [t105] (Seer) A random-pair result only compares two appearances. Before calling anyone cleared or caught, check the exceptions: the Katana holder reads Good that night, an Ex-Con reads Evil, and a framed player reads Evil. A DIFFERENT pair doesn't say which member is which. Spend your chosen-pair token on one anchored-Good player plus a suspect. - [t83] (Oracle) Guess the public role claims of suspicious or load-bearing players. Best is a freshly made or switched claim, above all a power claim made after a map or count put the player in a suspect group. Also good: defenders of exposed Yakuza, and cheap Vanilla claims. A WRONG exposes a fake at once, and nobody can say you copied it. A RIGHT on an already-public claim proves little to others, since a fake Oracle could invent it. So aim confirming guesses where they unlock another result: a RIGHT on one member of a census or group result lets elimination name the rest. Post a result as soon as it bears on a vote or a claim dispute; holding one that matters while others claim makes you the next suspect. The claim names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, so when one may be in the game a result that changes nothing yet can wait in your will. When you post, give every result in one message: night, target, exact role guessed, RIGHT or WRONG (e.g. 'N4: guessed Heidi = Tracker, WRONG'). A bare 'X is not a Tracker' sounds evasive. Your guess is Astral, so no 'exposure' excuse applies, but a swap still moves it: if your target posts a swap notice for that night, the result describes the other swapped player. Give a stated reason with every nomination, argue for a Vigilante shot in public rather than by PM, and keep your planned next guess in your will. Once confirmed, assume you die tonight and keep your will hedged. | support: 7 games, total weight 4.07 | recent evidence: Erin's WRONG on Frank = Surveyor got Frank hanged as a Diviner. Her RIGHT on Niaj = Ex-Con gained little.; Moderator: Alice spent N1 confirming Frank's own Vanilla claim, which gained little. His N1 and N4 guesses on low-information targets were wasted.; Grace: aim guesses at a claimed role in a live dispute (Mallory = Party Animal), not blindly at quiet players. That is why the Yakuza killed her to stop it.; Carol, Judy, Mallory, Olivia: guesses that only confirm public claims prove little, while a WRONG on a switched claim is decisive.

- [t117] (Archivist) Consult the archive early, while the living population and your sample are largest; waiting risks dying with the token unused. Report the full list, duplicates included, and remind the town how it is built. Of the N roles shown, floor(0.5 * N) are real roles of random players, and the rest are fakes drawn from all roles. So about half the list is fake, any single entry may be fake, a missing role may be present, and a role shown once can have several holders. Read it as odds, not a census. A dangerous entry (Arsonist, Serial Killer) should start a hunt, not be dismissed as a likely fake. Cross the list with confirmed claims and self-proving effects after every death to narrow the rest. Roles stay hidden on death, so a dead player's slot stays open unless their role was confirmed (e.g. a credible Coroner exam or Vigilante kill report). | support: 3 games, total weight 1.97 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.16: Bob: consulting on N1 left a list nobody could cross-check, and it was dismissed as unverifiable. - [t259] (Census Taker) Your count reads true alignment: the Katana holder's Good disguise, frames and Ex-Con readings don't fool it. Build a chain of cleared players. You pick only the first member; the other two are drawn at random from everyone else alive. Aim each census at one unknown: when both draws are already cleared, the count names that player exactly, and otherwise the group joins the chain for later subtraction. Post the group, the alignment counted and the count as soon as it bears on a vote or its members can confirm it. A Census Taker claim is a sure guess for a possible Ritualist (no Roleplayer is told it), so when one may be in the game write the full log in your will before you claim and expect to die that night. Neutrals are never counted, so phrase a gap as 'not Good' (Yakuza or Neutral), not 'Evil'. - [t250] (Coroner) Examine bodies whose role would settle a live question: one side of a duplicate claim, a hanged suspect (did the hang hit a Yakuza?), a victim whose will may be forged, or a dead claimant whose results the town relies on but whose claim is disputed. A Katana victim is almost never Yakuza, so examining an undisputed Katana victim rarely settles anything; push for today's hang instead. Post night, target and exact role the next Day in one message, and keep the log in your will. Your exam is Astral, so no visit log can confirm it, and your report is your word until it fits other evidence. - [t275] (Bloodhound) Each night you learn three names and one combined count: every Attack those three have made this game, tonight's included. It never says which of the three attacked, and it is a running total, so early trios set the baseline. A 0 means none of the three has attacked yet (a role-blocked attack doesn't count, and a Yakuza who hasn't held the Katana also reads 0). A positive count gives a pool of three. Narrow it by crossing trios across nights with each other, with 0 trios and with confirmed players, and remember that Village attackers (Vigilante shots, Veteran alerts, trap hits) count too. Spend scent tokens to put a quiet player who claims no night action (a Vanilla, an Oracle with only 'wrong' guesses) from the pool your earlier draws left into the trio. The other two are still random, so the count names that player only when both of them are already cleared. Don't spend a token on the loud disputed claimant: a 0 there can't tell a Jester or a non-holder Yakuza from a Villager. Post every trio and count with the night. Never say you will scent tonight: a named Bloodhound is a sure Ritualist guess. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.32 | recent evidence: Heidi's reflection. But while your claim is public and you still hold tokens, spend one on a Standard self-protection within the next night or two rather than dying with both. - [t167] (Trapper) A publicly announced trap on a confirmed investigator deters killers, who will route around that player, so it works like protection. But a lone holder simply avoids the predictable target, so a hidden trap is the one that actually catches someone: choose between deterrence and catching. Traps arm only from the next night, so aim at whoever the Katana is likely to hit in two nights. A silent trap also gives free negative info: report it as 'no non-Village visitor to X'. If a Lookout reveals your visit to a victim, claim at once with mechanics others can check. Any Trapper claim names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, so claim no earlier than a sighting or a vote forces it. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.96 | recent evidence: Judy announced a trap on Sheriff Grace; Rupert never attacked Grace, and Grace survived. - [t7] (Vigilante) Dying with unused tokens is the usual Vigilante failure: holding all your shots makes you a Vanilla Villager. Shoot on a lead, not on proof. Set a concrete trigger by Day 2-3 and fire a Basic shot (1 token) once it's met, and fire anyway by the time the Yakuza could be near parity. Good leads include a claim conflict, an Evil check against an unverifiable claim, a credible map or group result that leaves one unconfirmed member as the likely holder, or the stated votes and PM partners of a player shown Yakuza by a mechanical result (e.g. those who said they voted No on that player's nomination, argued to delay the hang, or whose stated votes the totals contradict). Roles are hidden on death and hard leads are scarce, so the strongest claimless voice is a fair target: someone who leads nominations on 'silence' or always echoes the consensus without results. A lone read/vote mismatch or one player's private request is not enough on its own; bring a PM'd request into public first. If you hit a hidden Cardinal, the curse Divine-attacks a random Good player. A Basic shot that fails on a suspect no credible Medic protects is evidence of innate Defense (Arsonist or Serial Killer Standard, Jester or Executioner Basic), so post it the next Day. If you already suspect a Neutral killer and want the kill, only a Strong shot (3 tokens) beats Standard Defense. If your shot kills, you learn the victim's role. That is one of the few ways a dead player's role becomes known, so post the night, target and role the next Day. Claim only once your shots have paid off: the claim names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, whose guess no Medic stops, so expect to die that night and fire any shot you still have a lead for. | support: 13 games, total weight 7.63 | against: 6 reports, total weight 2.75: Alice: Niaj followed the 'stay hidden and shoot' advice and shot Erin, the Surveyor, on a behavioral read. The moderator counts the miss as useful evidence.; Moderator (0.4) and Grace (0.16): Grace read the trigger too conservatively, never shot despite a mechanical map lead on Bob, and died on N5 with 3 tokens. She broke the 'don't wait' clause.; Moderator (0.4) and Heidi (0.16): the strict trigger again led to a Vigilante dying with every token. Misapplication, but the third such game. Frank, Heidi, Mallory and the moderator agreed; Carol filed 'hurt', but Frank broke the tip.; Moderator, Alice, Dave: Alice held fire until Erin's No on flipped Ivan's nomination plus Grace's Bob/Erin 'different' pair. Her Basic shot killed Erin (Yakuza) with one token spent.; Moderator and Mallory: Grace refused to shoot on Carol's private request, then her N6 Basic shot on Heidi failed to innate Defense. That failure helped hang the Arsonist on D7.; Moderator (0.4) and Carol (0.16): Carol held her shots 'for a solid lead' from D2 and died N4 with 3 tokens unused, while Alice openly claimed the Yakuza-only Diviner role.; Moderator, Alice, Erin, Grace, Heidi: Alice's first Basic shot on N3 killed Informant Bob, Frank's defender, the night the Katana killed her. - [t64] (Veteran) Once you claim Veteran publicly, Yakuza will avoid you, so later alerts mostly catch Village visitors (Medics, Lookouts) instead. Claim when it confirms a kill or saves a lynch, ideally after your best alerts are used. Never announce future alert nights or how many tokens you have left: it tells evil when you are safe to visit and only endangers your own side. When you do claim, give your full past alert record (each night, visitors or none) in one message and keep it fixed. A record that grows a night each Day looks invented even when it's true. Silence makes you the obvious unclaimed target for town investigators, and your alert will kill your own side. - [t103] (Prosecutor) Your claim is self-proving: the prosecution is announced the next Day, and Yakuza can't fake it. Stay unclaimed while your tokens are live. Spend each prosecution as soon as a solid mechanical lead exists, while enough Village voters are alive to win the trial. If you hold tokens for the endgame, the Katana removes your voters first and you may die with them unused. Good leads: the slip or PM partner of a player exposed by a mechanical result, a claim that overflows a public role list, a player in a confirmed visitor list, or a small Guilty total on a proven townie that the stated votes don't account for. Never spend one on a claimant whose results keep checking out (e.g. an Oracle with a verified hit). An acquittal wastes the token and one of the Day's three trials. A prosecution skips the nomination vote, so No votes can't stall it. Before each prosecution, name the target as a suspect in public that Day, but don't announce that you will prosecute tonight: a Pharmacist will block you. Log each prosecution in your will by NIGHT (N2, N3), not by trial day. If a copycat counterclaims, those earlier reads and correct labels are your proof. If you are role-blocked, you are told and no token is spent. If you have already claimed, post 'N3: prosecution of X was role-blocked' in your first message the next Day, before anyone can call the missing trial a bluff, and ask an Accountant to confirm your token count. If you are still unclaimed, keep the block in your will instead: a Prosecutor claim names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, whose guess no Medic stops. Under R21 the prosecution still fires even if you die that night, so spend your last token on your top suspect even when you expect to be killed. | support: 4 games, total weight 1.83 | recent evidence: Heidi's prosecution proved her claim against Frank, who was hanged on D4. Her prosecution of Erin led to Erin's hang on D5. Erin said the Yakuza should have killed the Prosecutor first.; Grace's prosecutions of Olivia and Ivan both flipped Yakuza, and her posthumous prosecution of Heidi won the game.; Judy kept both prosecution tokens unused all game and regretted not forcing a trial on her top lead.; Moderator and Alice: Alice claimed Prosecutor D4, held both tokens 'for a lead the Village agrees on' while naming Ivan herself; Ivan blocked her N5-N6 and she was killed N6 with both tokens unused.
- [t84] (Philanthropist) Give tokens only to roles verified by something other than their own claim. Best is a role confirmed by public effects whose token pays off by day, e.g. a Prosecutor whose announced prosecutions hit players later caught by mechanical results. Never announce whom you plan to gift. Don't gift a player likely to die that night, such as one who just announced a night action: a gift to a dead player confirms nothing and looks invented. Don't sit on both tokens waiting for certainty either. You're never told if the token was thrown away, so a fake claimer can just 'confirm' the gift, and your credibility gets tied to theirs. Your gift is a visit, so a claimed Fortune Teller or Cartographer must account for it, which can test their claim. A swap can redirect a gift without telling you. | support: 4 games, total weight 1.83 | recent evidence: Ivan announced a survey token for Frank. Frank 'confirmed' it, and once Frank flipped Diviner, Ivan looked like his partner and was hanged.; Judy and Grace: the gift to a verified Prosecutor was decisive.; Niaj gave Mallory a medic token, and she died N4 with 3 unspent tokens. Niaj says verify which claimant is real before gifting.; Moderator: Erin gave both tokens to Surveyor Carol, whose claim was corroborated. The extra tokens funded surveys, and Carol's will confirming the gifts cleared Erin on D4.
- [t115] (Party Animal) Choose blocks by the damage they prevent. Block the strongest Katana-holder suspect every night: a player a Lookout or Tracker saw at a victim, the last living name an elimination map allows, or late in the game the likeliest lone holder. Keep blocking that suspect every night until they are hanged. Switching away hands them a free kill, often on you. The Katana passes to a teammate when its holder dies, so re-check who likely holds it after every death. Before you have a suspect, use blocks as tests on claimed Vanillas and quiet players. A block that fails because the target can't be role-blocked shows an immune role (Pharmacist, Smuggler, Veteran or Party Animal). Post it with the moderator's exact wording. A no-kill night that matches your block is the town's strongest lead against a lone holder. Write each block target in your will before the night. Post the log publicly, before anyone can confirm it, once it bears on a claim, a no-kill night or a vote: a target who later confirms being blocked proves you are real. Posting names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, whose guess no Medic stops, so when one may be in the game don't post a log that shows nothing yet. Never block a protector or investigator confirmed by an independent source, and avoid blocking quiet players who defended confirmed claims, who may be a hidden Vigilante. A persistent liar who keeps showing no visits may be an Executioner, and blocking them prevents nothing. | support: 5 games, total weight 3.71 | recent evidence: Alice's reflection on blocking Frank (Executioner) versus the Katana carriers.; Moderator: after Heidi tracked Niaj to Frank on the Katana night, Peggy blocked Niaj on N4 and no kill happened.; Niaj blocked the Katana holder on N3-N5, stopping every kill. He notes the town should then hang the blocked holder by day.; Moderator, Mallory and others: Mallory blocked Heidi on N2 (ballot token lost) and N3 (holder, no kill), then Frank on N4 (holder, no kill). The no-kill nights became key evidence in both hangs.; Mallory: write your N1 block target in your will before Night 1. Olivia confirming the block was all that saved Mallory's log.
- [t225] (Ex-Con) The first time you're pressed for your night target, claim Ex-Con in one plain sentence. Say you visit a random player every night, aren't told whom, and read Evil to alignment checks. That explains a missing target, any Lookout or Tracker sighting of you at a victim, and a DIFFERENT Seer pair or Evil result. Ask for a Census Taker check: the census counts true alignment, so it is the one tool that clears you. Keep a will that maps where Cartographer, FT and Lookout data place your random visit each night, so sightings of you aren't misread. Evasive 'I can't name a target' answers build a case for your trial. The claim does name your exact role to a possible Ritualist, so don't volunteer it before you are pressed or read Evil; once you are, evasion costs more than the claim. | support: 2 games, total weight 1.40 | recent evidence: Moderator and Erin: Erin dodged for five rounds and was nominated 9-2. Once she claimed Ex-Con on trial, the claim fit Judy's pair and the archive, and she was acquitted 11-0.; Ivan: claim early and note in your will that the census counts an Ex-Con as Good.
- [t147] (Cardinal) Stay hidden while safe. Your curse hits only Good players, so to the Yakuza you are a two-for-one kill. Claim Vanilla publicly if pressed, but put the true Cardinal claim in your will so the town reads the random Good death correctly. Never vote Guilty on a nominee who might be a Jester or an Executioner's target: if the revenge draw hits you, your curse kills a second Good player. Vote Innocent, say why, and let others carry the hang. If a Vigilante may shoot you or the Village is about to hang you, claim Cardinal at once. The claim names your exact role to a possible Ritualist, whose Divine guess no protection stops, so make it only when the alternative is a Village kill. Once an archive or survey exposes you anyway, ask openly for protection, because the Yakuza now want you dead. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.36 | recent evidence: Grace was shot by Village Vigilante Dave on a vote-mismatch read. Her curse killed Alice and Frank, dropping the game to 5 alive.; Dave: record the curse in your will so the extra death isn't misread. His curse killed Frank, the last Good player, after the ignite.
- [t36] (Pharmacist) Blocking your kill target stops their action, not the Medic protection coming in. Putting your block and the Katana on one target hides a destination from Trackers, but it wastes the kill on an obvious protection target. After a failed kill, block the likely Medic (or the Vigilante or investigator) while the Katana hits someone less protected. If you also hold the Katana, you are block-immune. A claimed investigator is a prime Lookout watch target, so vary your blocks and have a cover action ready. | support: 3 games, total weight 0.99 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: The 'pair your block with the Katana kill' advice backfired. Good targets are a claimed Seer or Lookout whose next result could expose the team, or the likely Medic on a kill night. An unused block is a free night for the town's investigators. | support: 3 games, total weight 0.72 | recent evidence: Ivan never blocked anyone all game. - [t242] (Pharmacist) You can't be role-blocked, and a Village blocker whose block on you fails is told it failed on immunity. Party Animals test claimed Vanillas this way. Don't hide behind Vanilla. Before Day 2, agree in night chat on a cover that explains immunity, 0 tokens and a visit each night. Party Animal fits all three, but only while no real Party Animal has claimed or posted blocks. Never name your real role, even loosely ('Pharmacist-type'). Each block you land is told to its target. - [t124] (Ritualist) Hold your tokens until elimination makes an exact role near-certain. A wrong guess is announced at the start of the next Day, naming you as a Ritualist, and its tokens are spent either way; only a role-blocked guess keeps them. - [t134] (Ritualist) A hard-claimed investigator is a sure guess, and a 2-token Divine guess goes through any Medic. With no sign of a Medic, one token (Standard) is enough and leaves you a second kill. Prefer first-turn claims of roles a Roleplayer can't be told (not Sheriff, Tracker, Lookout, Seer, Fortune Teller, Oracle or Accountant) whose posted results match facts your team knows are true. A Roleplayer may sit behind a Roleplayer-list claim, and a miss outs you publicly with the tokens spent anyway. A swap moves your guess too (R23): never guess a player your Smuggler is swapping that night, because the guess lands on the other player. Sharp villagers know it is the obvious Ritualist cover, so keep the fake log on living, uncontroversial players and never on Katana victims. Save one fake 'guessed X = their claimed role, WRONG' for the endgame, to sink a real villager who shares a role claim with a teammate. - [t72] (Puppeteer) Puppet a trusted villager on the day you need a swing, ideally the same Day as a Clerk rig, and push a mis-hang subtly in their voice. Kill them that night, and have the Mortician clean them, before they can expose it. - [t42] (Fabricator) Save the forge token for a night your target is actually likely to die (e.g. the Katana target when no protector is suspected). Roles stay hidden on death, so the town reads a forged will as the victim's own claim. It still checks the will against what the victim said while alive and against living claimants and investigators. A role claim the victim never made, or one a living counterclaimant disputes, exposes the forge and the frame. Forge something vague that fits the victim's public record. A forged will must fit the public transcript, and invented chronology others can check exposes you. Don't spend a frame on someone who already reads Evil (Jester, Executioner, Ex-Con). Keep your own will matching your public claim, and don't let it copy a teammate's claim word for word. | support: 2 games, total weight 0.58 | recent evidence: Dave spent his forge on N1 on Alice, but Medic Frank protected her. - [t214] (Smuggler) Spend your swap tokens on the nights they protect the team: when a Cartographer, Lookout or Tracker is likely to catch the Katana holder, when a Vigilante shot or trap may hit a teammate, or to redirect a kill onto a protected target's neighbor. Swapping the holder with a non-Yakuza moves every action aimed at the holder, Astral checks included (an Oracle guess, a Juror pair, a map), so plan it in night chat. It moves your own team's actions as well: a Ritualist guess aimed at a swapped player lands on the other. A swap is a visit by you to both players, adds to Fortune Teller counts and map groups, and both players are told. So while a claimed visit counter or Lookout is alive, have a cover ready or wait. - [t58] (Ballot Stuffer) Do the trial math in night chat. Spend a token only on a Day when your doubled vote can change an outcome, e.g. near half: a doubled Innocent makes trials need more Guilty than the Village has (7 alive means 8 counted, so 5 Guilty are needed). That blocks every execution, even of an exposed teammate. Holding tokens 'for a later close trial' is the usual failure. Once a teammate has been read Evil or hanged, expect the next close trial to be on your team, possibly on you, and spend a token the night before it. A bare-majority hang (4 of 7) becomes a failing tie with your extra vote. Nomination votes aren't doubled. Vote the way you said you would publicly: the extra vote narrows suspects to voters on that side, and a stance/ballot mismatch names you. | support: 6 games, total weight 3.92 | recent evidence: Moderator, Mallory and Bob: Mallory's N2 token turned D3 into a 4-4 lock, and the exposed Grace survived.; Erin planned a doubled Innocent with three Yakuza alive so that no trial could pass. Use them on a close trial or the token is wasted.; Niaj: doubling only matters on a trial it can swing. Save it for the deciding Day.; Heidi: the margins (11-1, 9-1, 7-1) were too wide for a doubled vote to swing anything.; Alice: she held tokens while Village majorities were large and spent one the night before the Day the team could reach parity. - [t253] (Fixer) Before each night, count the likely Guilty votes on any trial that could hang a teammate. If fixing one pro-hang voter breaks the Guilty majority (a tie acquits), spend a token on that voter the night before. The trial on your own exposed teammate, or on yourself, is the trial that matters, so don't hoard tokens for a later one. Pick a voter who has said they will vote Guilty, since the fix flips the vote they actually cast. Offensively, at or near parity, fix a Villager likely to vote Innocent the night before the first close trial of a non-teammate. Their own Innocent then counts Guilty and can turn a narrow acquittal into a hang. The totals won't match that player's stated vote, so suspicion falls on them, not you. Agree in night chat that nobody fixes a player the Katana or Ritualist targets that same night: a dead voter casts nothing, and the token is wasted. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.56 | recent evidence: Moderator (0.4), Frank (0.16): Frank was read Evil on N1 and the D2 nomination drew 4 Yes. He held both tokens and was hanged 5-3 on D3. One fixed Guilty would have made it 4-4 and saved him.
- [t81] (Diviner) Your results look exactly like a Surveyor's, so informed towns treat a Surveyor claim as the Diviner's twin. Accurate hits don't clear it. An Oracle guess on your claim or a profile of you breaks it (your true role is always on your own profile list), a Diviner entry on an archive or on someone else's profile points the town at it, and an Accountant sees your 0 tokens against a Surveyor's 3. Prefer a tokenless visiting cover (Lookout, Tracker, Sheriff) that names your real divine targets as its targets, and share your draws with the team in night chat instead of publishing them. If you do use Surveyor, publish a few accurate, harmless groups early and put a plausible other suspect in front of the town. Divine teammates who must visit risky targets: Strong Defense stops traps and Vigilante shots. A token gifted to you is thrown away. | support: 3 games, total weight 2.56 | against: 1 reports, total weight 1.00: Frank, Niaj, Olivia: the Surveyor cover that t81 recommends was predictable and exposed by a Diviner profile decoy plus a census. - [t172] (Diviner) (Diviner) Picking yourself gives you Strong Defense for the night but no reading; do it once you hold the Katana and expect a block or attack. - [t53] (Mortician) A clean token is spent even if the target survives. Roles are hidden on death, so a clean hides the victim's last will: it shows as 'cleaned by mortician', and you privately learn their role and will. It also blocks a Coroner, who learns nothing from a cleaned body. The public notice proves a Mortician exists. Clean only when the kill is near-certain (no possible Defense, no likely protector) and the will or body likely holds something that hurts your team: an investigator who has probably checked you, a puppeting notice, or one side of a claim duel a Coroner would examine. A clean of a player whose results are already public saves little. Don't add your clean visit to another Yakuza's Katana target while a Lookout may watch; clean only when you hold the Katana yourself or the visit is expendable. | support: 4 games, total weight 0.66 | recent evidence: Niaj's reflection.; Mallory: clean only targets sure to die. Her clean on Alice landed and hid Alice's Oracle info.; Mallory: don't spend a clean on a target the Katana already failed on. Clean only likely deaths.; Heidi: Carol's cleans on Dave, who was protected and survived, gave nothing and wasted tokens.
- [t68] (Wiretap) Stay quiet and never hint publicly that a Wiretap exists. The Village's own fear of a Wiretap often shuts down PM evidence for you. Never quote or closely paraphrase a PM: a quote proves you read it, and the two people in that exchange will ask how. Use what you read to steer votes and your team's night choices without revealing content. If you must use it, have an Eavesdropper cover ready with a named previous-night target who took part in that exchange (see t119). If the table sends no PMs, you are effectively a Grunt, so put your effort into a visit-ready cover claim and vote steering. Don't take the Katana as the team's 'low-value holder' while a Fortune Teller, Cartographer or Lookout is active, unless your Day 2 cover explains a visit to the victim. A no-visit cover plus a Katana edge forces a fatal claim switch. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.64 | recent evidence: Grace: the town's 'no PMs because of Wiretap' warning killed PM evidence. Her teammates' PMs, not her, were the risk.

- [t97] (Vulture) Claim a plain, tokenless no-action role early (Vanilla works when no Oracle or exact-role checker is active, and it matches an audit of 0) and keep it word for word. Vote with the majority and never lead a push or nominate: both killing factions then leave you for last, and the count runs down to your threshold by itself. Avoid vouching for players, lone Guilty votes and aggressive endgame nominations, especially of a Cardinal. Count the threshold, max(floor(N/4),2) alive at the start of a Day, after every death. - [t13] (Arsonist) Ignite is a Strong Astral attack. It is invisible to Lookouts, Trackers, Insomniacs and Fortune Teller counts, but it fails against Strong Defense (a Medic's Strong token protection, a Diviner's Strong Defense on a Yakuza). An ignite uses up every douse, so a player who survives it is no longer doused and must be doused again before a later ignite. Ignite when your douses are unlikely to be protected. Doused Yakuza and Neutrals are told an Arsonist doused them; Good players are not. Douse is a normal visit, so don't douse a player a Tracker or Lookout is likely watching or has publicly committed to. | support: 1 games, total weight 0.63 | recent evidence: Niaj's own analysis.

- [t92] (Jester) Build a hard, Village-sounding case. A fake investigative counterclaim against a credible info role (e.g. a fake Tracker result contradicting a Lookout's list) is a fast route to a hang. A passive counting claim (Cartographer, Fortune Teller) with a bold, falsifiable result posted before anyone speaks ('one visit all night') works well: the real visitors contradict you, and the town reads a duel, not a Jester. You needn't hang on Day 2. Escalate into a specific accusation. If a Villager hangs on it, the night's events break your claim and the town hangs the 'proven liar' the next Day. Being the obvious next hang also keeps the Katana off you, which your Basic Defense wouldn't stop. Commit to the claim and reject the Roleplayer excuse the town offers. On trial, fight: accuse your accusers and say you vote Innocent while voting Guilty. One small checkable lie (a secret Yes on your own nomination, stated as No) tips waverers, but careful players read it as a Jester tell, so don't overdo it. A self-nomination framed as a 'test' can also work, but repeated self-nominations or an open Jester claim make the town refuse to hang you. | support: 6 games, total weight 1.31 | against: 1 reports, total weight 0.17: Judy and Niaj: repeated self-nominations and an open Jester claim made the town refuse to hang Judy, and she lost. Build a real Village-side case instead. | recent evidence: Frank pushed Carol on D1, self-nominated on D2 and was hanged 11-0 for the win.; Grace (won): she claimed the Medic role from Heidi's survey pool, the real Medic counterclaimed with a log, and the town hanged her 9-1.; Moderator and Frank: Frank self-nominated six times and asked Dave by PM for a Yes vote. Dave called it a Jester pattern on D1, the Oracle confirmed it, and no nomination of Frank got more than 4 Yes votes.; Frank: repeated self-nominations after his confessed lie drew Innocent votes from everyone. - [t145] (Jester) Don't play clean. Voting with confirmed town and claiming Vanilla gives you no route to a hanging, and a town with confirmed power roles will hang real Evil instead of you. Build suspicion by midgame with odd votes, a shaky but committed claim or an aggressive push, so you look plausibly Yakuza. Don't confess a fake claim and then self-nominate again and again: that marks you as a Jester, and the table refuses your trials for days. - [t170] (Jester) Make yourself the town's concrete lead. One short early PM to a single pushy or quiet player becomes a link if they're later exposed as Yakuza. On trial, claim Vanilla (it matches your 0 tokens), give a short, weak defense that blames the accuser, and vote Guilty on yourself (ballots are secret, so it only shows in the total).
